--- Post 1 ---
Author: sohansethi
Date: May 17, 2025
Engagement: 32,010 reactions, 491 comments, 755 reposts (Score: 166,470)
Media: image
URL: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7307024396173357056

This Resume got me interviews at Google, Amazon, JPMorganChase, Capital One, CVS Health and many more!
Here's how you can get them too:

Your resume is your entry ticket to a job interview,
And with the right resume, you can open some great interview opportunities for yourself.

Here’s exactly how you should structure a good resume:

1. Education:
-- Let the hiring manager know the knowledge that you’ve been trained for.

2. Skills:
-- Convey the skills that you can contribute to the team.

3. Work Experience:
-- Showcase how your previous experience will help you successfully carry out the responsibilities of the role that you’re interviewing for.

4. Projects:
This section helps you portray your expertise in a broad range of skill sets.

5. Leadership and Awards:
Portraying a holistic personality further adds to the credibility of you as a candidate.

Having your resume structured this way will be the easiest way for the hiring manager to select you as a potential candidate and send out an interview invite.

In addition, here are a few tips for a fully optimized resume:

-- If you have less than 10 years of experience, always keep a 1-page resume.
-- Make sure to include tons of metrics and data to support your achievements
-- Always format your bullet like this: Accomplished [X] as measured by [Y], by doing [Z].
-- Tailor a unique resume for each job application to make it ATS-friendly.

If you are currently struggling to get callbacks, try these suggestions.
I assure you, that by implementing them for just 2-3 weeks, you’ll see an increase in the number of callbacks that you get.

My aim with this post is to help everyone currently in the job market to excel in their job search process.

You can create a similar ATS friendly resume using Wisedoc AI Recruiting
Link: https://www.wisedoc.net/

Feel free to use and take inspiration from my resume below.
I hope you get an interview call soon, All the best!

If you found this post useful, then please:
👍 Like

♻ Reshare

--- Post 8 ---
Author: sohansethi
Date: May 17, 2025
Engagement: 5,565 reactions, 393 comments, 637 reposts (Score: 33,029)
Media: image
URL: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7311736752405168128

Give me 2 mins and I’ll save you 6 months in your job search:
(With the help of free resources below!)

The below list is compiled from every resource I've followed during my own job search process over the last 3 years.
I hope it helps you receive more callbacks and get your next offer!

1. Top 10 LinkedIn Creators to follow:
a. Adam Broda - Follow for job search strategies
b. Alex Freberg - Follow for learning data analytics
c. Kumud Deepali Rudraraju, SHRM-CP - Follow for finding jobs abroad
d. Gina Acosta Gutiérrez - Follow for learning data science
e. Aditya Sharma - Follow for data science tips
f. Venkata Naga Sai Kumar Bysani - Follow for learning data analytics
g. Reno Perry - Follow for job search advice
h. Sundas Khalid - Follow for career growth
i. Sahil Gaba- Follow for tech interview preparation
j. Basant Shenouda - Follow for job search tips

Their advice helped me immensely to navigate my interview process better.

2. AI Resume Builders to tailor your Resume:
a. Jobright.ai - https://lnkd.in/gg46pjYc
b. Jobscan - www.jobscan.co
c. Loopcv - www.loopcv.pro

These tools helped me improve my resume over time during my job search process.

3. Learning Platforms:
a. Dataquest.io - www.dataquest.io
b. DataCamp - www.datacamp.com
c. GeeksforGeeks - www.geeksforgeeks.org

Learning data analytics concepts through these platforms helped me better prepare for interviews.

4. Websites for Internships and Job Search:
a. LinkedIn - www.linkedin.com
b. Indeed - www.indeed.com
c. Built In - https://builtin.com/
d. ZipRecruiter - www.ziprecruiter.com

I primarily applied through these 4 websites to diversify my pool of applications.

5. Assistance with Job Applications:
a. Simplify - https://simplify.jobs/

It was an incredibly useful tool to reduce the time I spend on my applications drastically.

6. Interview Preparation:
a. Kaggle
b. LeetCode
c. HackerRank

Practicing technical questions through these platforms helped me build confidence and become more proficient over time.

7. Networking:
a. Luma - https://lu.ma/
b. Meetup - www.meetup.com

Attending events through these platforms helped me get leads and referrals during my job search process.

To sum it up, I believe your job search process will be one of the hardest things that you will ever go through.
However, I hope this post helps make it a little bit easier by providing those key resources.

The best thing about tough and uncertain times is, that they eventually go away.
I'm sure you'll find your next interview is just around the corner.

Hope this was helpful. Keep growing and take care!

♻ Repost and share this post, maybe someone needs to desperately read this.
💬 Also, comment below if you find any one of the tips useful.

P.S: I post FREE job search tips and resources. Connect with me for more such resources daily.

--- Post 10 ---
Author: sohansethi
Date: May 17, 2025
Engagement: 5,235 reactions, 170 comments, 57 reposts (Score: 27,989)
Media: image
URL: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7254145428852486145

Unfortunately you were not selected, due to lack of visa sponsorship.
Unfortunately you were not selected, due to lack of visa sponsorship.
Unfortunately you were not selected, due to lack of visa sponsorship.

Most of us have often come across this response in an email.

However, is it just an email or a major life setback?
How many International Students have lost their dreams due to visa issues?

I know someone:

— Who applied to 10000+ jobs and still did not get an offer due to lack of sponsorship
— Who did not get selected in multiple visa attempts and left the country
— Who faced a layoff and had to leave the country with his family
— Who has multiple people dependent on him and is fearing a layoff anytime soon

I totally understand economics and civil rights, however I also believe that all international students in all countries who come through a fair and competitive process should be given a better chance too.

My request to all employers would be this:
Almost all international students are highly sincere and dedicated to their work, and they are excellent employees, too. 

Please give them a fair chance as they have a lot on the line:
From Student loans to family pressures, the list keeps on going.

A Small yes can have a big impact on someone’s life.

I hope the job market improves soon. Wishing a ton of success to the international student community.

Do you agree?

Let me know your thoughts in the comments below👇

Using this CV will help you get interviews with FAANG:

Your resume is your entry ticket to a job interview,
And with the right resume, you can open some great interview opportunities for yourself.

Here’s exactly how you should structure a good resume:

1. Education:
-- Let the hiring manager know the knowledge that you’ve been trained for.

2. Skills:
-- Convey the skills that you can contribute to the team.

3. Work Experience:
-- Showcase how your previous experience will help you successfully carry out the responsibilities of the role that you’re interviewing for.

4. Projects:
This section helps you portray your expertise in a broad range of skill sets.

5. Leadership and Awards:
Portraying a holistic personality further adds to the credibility of you as a candidate.

Having your resume structured this way will be the easiest way for the hiring manager to select you as a potential candidate and send out an interview invite.

In addition, here are a few tips for a fully optimized resume:

-- If you have less than 10 years of experience, always keep a 1-page resume.
-- Make sure to include tons of metrics and data to support your achievements
-- Always format your bullet like this: Accomplished [X] as measured by [Y], by doing [Z].
-- Tailor a unique resume for each job application to make it ATS-friendly.

Your resume is your entry ticket to a job interview,
And with the right resume, you can open some great interview opportunities for yourself.

Here’s exactly how you should structure a good resume:

1. Education:
-- Let the hiring manager know the knowledge that you’ve been trained for.

2. Skills:
-- Convey the skills that you can contribute to the team.

3. Work Experience:
-- Showcase how your previous experience will help you successfully carry out the responsibilities of the role that you’re interviewing for.

4. Projects:
This section helps you portray your expertise in a broad range of skill sets.

5. Leadership and Awards:

Having your resume structured this way will be the easiest way for the hiring manager to select you as a potential candidate and send out an interview invite.

-- Make sure to include tons of metrics and data to support your achievements
-- Always format your bullet like this: Accomplished [X] as measured by [Y], by doing [Z].
-- Tailor a unique resume for each job application to make it ATS-friendly.

Finally, it feels that all the sacrifices have been worth it:
— Staying 3.5 years away from my family and home
— Working 15 hrs/day, juggling college and part-time jobs
— 5000+ applications and an exhausting job search journey
— Overcoming loneliness, anxiety, and re-building myself from scratch

This breathtaking journey wouldn't have been possible without the incredible support system around me.

First and foremost, I’d like to express my sincere gratitude to Brad Montella for being extremely supportive throughout the interview and for a flawless onboarding process. I'm excited about this new chapter, and I am grateful for this opportunity. I assure you, together we’ll make an impact in our space in the years to come.

Next, I am incredibly thankful for Max Barron, PMP, Bridget Kukis, PMP, Mackenzie Meyer, and Justin Capp who saw potential in me and provided me with opportunities to grow. Your guidance has been invaluable. Special thanks to Max for being the captain of my ship, your consistent support and selfless guidance truly helped me grow.

A special thank you to Bhavya Passi, whose unwavering support, patience, and belief in me have been my backbone throughout this journey. I couldn't have done this without you. I am also grateful to Darshan Nandekar and Marc O Goodman, everything I have learnt from you has been instrumental in my professional success.

Lastly, thanks to my Mom, my Brother, and my inner circle of friends. Your constant support and belief in me brings out the best in me, even when it's too dark for me to see my own light.

In the end, I am incredibly grateful for my LinkedIn community. Each one of you made me the best version of myself and helped me spread my light with the world.

My message to anyone feeling stuck in their journey is this:
1. Your struggles don't define your potential
2. Your journey doesn't have to match anyone else's timeline
3. Your breakthrough might be just around the corner

Remember: The same challenges that feel overwhelming today will be the foundation of your success story tomorrow.

Finally, Thank you to everyone for being a part of this boy’s journey. The man today is grateful to be a part of yours!

Keep Believing, Keep growing!

--- Post 18 ---
Author: sohansethi
Date: May 17, 2025
Engagement: 2,538 reactions, 113 comments, 12 reposts (Score: 13,844)
Media: image
URL: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7265545389779427328

2 Bags, 3 Months of Rent and $50,000 in Debt,
That's all I brought to the USA with me 3 years ago

The initial excitement soon faded when loneliness hit me hard!

Before I knew it, every day was more of a survival and less of a glamorous experience abroad.

The 1st one year was filled with:
— 15-hour days juggled between part-time jobs and studies
— Cleaning tables and wiping floors to earn $13/hr
— Virtual birthdays, weddings, and missing your loved ones back home
— Exhausting job-search process with 3000+ applications (and 99% rejections)

However, things soon changed near graduation:
— I started to get my confidence back
— Got my dream job in a great company
— Built a 45,000+ community on LinkedIn (this I am most grateful for)
— Featured on a billboard at Times Square in NYC

Having said that, this post is not about me. My aim with this post is to convey an important message to the reader (To you, yes you beautiful human reading this)

My Message: 
In my darkest days, feeling lost and defeated, I never imagined I'd build this platform that I am grateful for today. 

It was only those struggles that shaped me. 

If you’re stuck now, remember that every setback is a setup for an incredible future. Life’s toughest seasons often lead to the most beautiful breakthroughs. 

If I could do this, I have even more faith you.
Your story will indeed be a remarkable one!

Take this post as a reminder that:
1. You are bigger than all your problems.
2. You are special in your own beautiful way.
3. Everything you are going through will soon be worth it!

If you found this post relatable, then please like and comment your thoughts below.

Maybe your support can help reach it to someone who desperately needs to hear this today!

Keep Believing, keep helping, keep growing!

P.S: The picture on the left is me right after getting my U.S visa approved. 
The right one is from a recent trip to Michigan.

I feel like a totally different person after 3 years. What do you think?

============================================================

Author: sohansethi

I’m happy to share that I am starting my new job as a Data Analyst at Google:

“It was no small task, but I finally did it.
I am proud of this achievement,
However, it was obvious that I was always a high achiever since childhood:

➡ I was among the toppers in my school.
➡ I got a great rank in my engineering entrance exams
➡ I excelled and graduated from IIT Bombay
➡ I worked for Microsoft India
➡ I came to the US and graduated from Harvard.
➡ Now finally, I got into the world’s biggest company: Google”

Alright, I know all this sounds really narcissistic, however, this is inspired by a recent post I saw last week on LinkedIn.

By now you might have known that I am not starting any new position at Google. I have not gone to either IIT or Harvard.

I always support celebrating your achievements, yet boasting about them without any human sentiments is not cool at all.

You never know what an individual might be going through:
-- Someone might be struggling to get a job.
-- Someone might be working at a job that they absolutely hate.
-- Someone’s job might be great, but their personal life might be full of misery.

No one has it figured out, and that's okay.

99.99% of LinkedIn users never went to any prestigious institutions and a self-centered post like the one above shouldn’t cause anxiety and self-doubt for someone already going through a lot.

I have tons of respect for people who show extraordinary hard work and achieve such great results. 
However, I have tons of respect for someone starting out and failing countless times to secure an entry-level positions in a tiny startup too.

It’s the effort and intention that eventually counts.
Keep putting in the work and the right opportunity will soon be yours!

Do you agree?

If you found this relatable, please:
👍 Like
🗯 Comment

Author: sohansethi
Date: Jun 21, 2025
Engagement: 444 reactions, 802 comments, 9 reposts (Score: 10,258)
Media: image
URL: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7338923268696940544

This Cover Letter got me interviews at Google, Amazon, CVS Health, Capital One, Goldman Sachs, and many more!

A cover letter is what separates you the 250+ other equally qualified candidates.

And with the right approach, you can turn it into your secret weapon for increasing number of callbacks.

Here's exactly how a winning cover letter is structured:

1. Opening Hook: 
-- Start with a compelling first line that grabs attention immediately. 
-- Mention the specific role and where you found it.

2. The Connection: 
-- Research the company and show genuine interest in their mission. 
-- Reference a recent company achievement, news, or initiative.

3. Your Value Proposition: 
-- Highlight 2-3 key achievements that directly relate to the job requirements. 
-- Use specific metrics and results to back up your claims.

4. The Bridge: 
-- Explain how your past experience translates to success in this specific role. 
-- Address any potential concerns or gaps proactively.

5. Strong Close: 
-- Express enthusiasm and request next steps. 
-- Include a professional sign-off with your contact information.

Here are the game-changing tips that make cover letters irresistible:

— Keep it to 3-4 paragraphs maximum. Hiring managers spend 6 seconds scanning it. 
-- Use the same keywords from the job posting to beat ATS systems. 
-- Follow this formula: "In my previous role at [Company], I [specific achievement with numbers], which resulted in [quantifiable impact]." 
-- Always customize for each application - generic cover letters often get ignored.

The Secret Sauce: Research the hiring manager on LinkedIn and address them by name. This simple step increases your response rate by 40%.

The cover letter template I used was optimized with insights from Wisedoc AI Recruiting 
You can access their proven templates and AI-powered customization tools below
Link: https://wisedoc.net/

If you're currently getting rejected without interviews, implement these strategies. 
I assure that within 2-3 weeks using this method, you'll start seeing interview invitations.

📍BONUS: Comment your Email ID below and I’ll send you my own cover letter that got me multiple FAANG Interviews.

Feel free to Reshare this post to help others and use these strategies in your next application. 
Your dream job interview is just one great cover letter away!

The email that changes everything: 
'Unfortunately, your employment at 
hashtag
#Meta is being terminated!'

Meta is laying off 3,600 employees i.e. 5% of its workforce.
Many laid-off employees have just 60 days to find a new job before their visa expires.

Here's what you can do to land a new role in 45 days:

1. The First 24 Hours: 
-- Don’t panic or make rushed applications 
-- Updated your LinkedIn profile with "Open to Work" 
-- Reach out to your professional network 
-- Make a structured job search plan

2. The Application Strategy (Do this instead of mass applying):
-- Create a list of visa-sponsoring companies 
-- Focused on similar roles where your skills are transferred 
-- Tailored your resume with ATS-friendly keywords 
-- Set a daily target of 15 quality applications.

3. The Network Approach: 
-- Message former colleagues and managers 
-- Connected with alumni from your university 
-- Join layoff support groups 
-- Reach out to recruiters with a clear message

4. The Mental Game: 
-- Treat your job search like a 9-5 workday 
-- Take breaks to avoid burnout 
-- Join a gym to manage stress or go on long walks 
-- Maintain a routine despite uncertainty

What Nobody Tells You About Layoffs:
1. Many Meta engineers found better roles after previous layoffs
2. Some started successful businesses
3. Others discovered better work-life balance
4. Several found higher pay at smaller companies

To every Meta engineer reading this: Your career is bigger than one company. Sometimes the most painful transitions lead to the best opportunities.

If you're affected by layoffs:
1. Take care of your mental health first
2. Leverage the Meta name while it's fresh
3. Use your severance time strategically
4. Connect with others in the same situation

If you're in the middle of a job search, keep going. The only way past this is through this.
Keep pushing forward. The tech industry still needs your talent.

My network and all my support is with laid-off Meta employees.
Share and Comment on this post to help it reach someone in need!

-- Make sure to include tons of metrics and data to support your achievements
-- Always format your bullet like this: Accomplished [X] as measured by [Y], by doing [Z].
-- Tailor a unique resume for each job application to make it ATS-friendly.

If you are currently struggling to get callbacks, try these suggestions.
I assure you, that by implementing them for just 2-3 weeks, you’ll see an increase in the number of callbacks that you get.

My aim with this post is to help everyone currently in the job market to excel in their job search process.

I created this optimized resume with the help of Wisedoc AI Recruiting
You can create a similar ATS friendly resume using their AI Tool too!
Link: https://wisedoc.net/

Feel free to use and take inspiration from my resume below.
I hope you get an interview call soon, All the best!

--- Post 31 ---
Author: sohansethi
Date: May 17, 2025
Engagement: 1,579 reactions, 137 comments, 6 reposts (Score: 9,277)
Media: image
URL: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7285306208855498752

Coming to the USA for my master’s was the WORST decision of my life,
It has been the most painful choice I’ve made:

— I am always worried about finding a job
— I am always worried about getting an H1B visa
— I am always worried about my huge student loan
— I am always worried about making ends meet

If this wasn’t enough, I am constantly anxious because:

— I haven’t seen my family for years
— I miss the support of my childhood friends
— I love the comfort of life back home

These were the thoughts running through my head almost a year ago.

However, instead of succumbing to these thoughts at the peak of my job-search process, a sudden shift happened in my life:

I realized I was responsible for my own future, and no one was coming to save me!
This is when I became proactive.

These are the strategies I followed to manage stress and keep moving forward:

1. Set realistic goals:
Instead of trying to apply to 50 jobs in one day, I aimed for 15 high-quality applications.

2. Created a routine:
I treated my job search like a job. I allocated specific hours each day for networking, applying, and upskilling.

3. Celebrated small wins:
Celebrate small milestones because they’re signs of progress too.

4. Practice self-care:
I set aside time for exercise, hobbies, or simply relaxing with a book or my favorite show.

5. Seeked support:
Reach out to mentors, friends, or connections — your network is always there to support you.

Remember, stress is natural, but it doesn’t have to derail your journey.
And just like everything else, it is temporary too!

It was only after facing these tough situations that I ultimately:

— Got my dream job in a great U.S company
— Was featured on a billboard at Times Square in NYC
— Built a 50,000+ community on LinkedIn (this I am most grateful for)

If I had the chance, I would go through that pain all over again.

I am sure many of you have felt like how I was feeling just a year ago.
If you’re stuck now, remember that every setback is a setup for an incredible future.

Life’s toughest seasons often lead to the most beautiful breakthroughs.

If I could do this, I have even more faith in you.
Your story will indeed be a remarkable one!

Take this post as a reminder that:
1. You are bigger than all your problems.
2. You are special in your own beautiful way.
3. Everything you are going through will soon be worth it!

If you found this post relatable, then please like and comment your thoughts below.

Maybe your support can help it reach someone who desperately needs to hear this today!

Keep Believing, keep helping, keep growing!

============================================================

--- Post 32 ---
Author: sohansethi
Date: May 17, 2025
Engagement: 1,458 reactions, 83 comments, 5 reposts (Score: 8,130)
Media: image
URL: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7274816968015114246

My LinkedIn profile says I'm working as a Business Analyst in the USA and living my dream life,
Yet I will say, It’s all a LIE!

Because, my LinkedIn doesn’t say that:
-- There was a time when I was jobless and had more than $50,000 in student loan debt.

-- There was a time when I missed major festivals, friend’s marriages, and parents’ birthdays back home.

-- There was a time when I was wiping tables and cleaning dishes as a part-time student.

-- There was a time when I felt out of place and had difficulty making new friends in a foreign country.

-- There was a time when I was stressed for months and faced endless rejections during my job search period.

Life as an international student is at times full of hard work, anxiety and unexpected struggles.

Thing's might seem sorted right now, however, just a year ago my life was filled with uncertainty.
I was about to graduate, didn't have a job in hand and was thinking how will I pay my bills from the next few months.

The fear of paying rent, managing living expenses and preparing for unexpected circumstances was definitely tough.
Moreover, having a feeling of not being good enough while at the same time seeing everybody else do better than me was surely the most anxious thing of all.

However, while I was feeling all these emotions, I realized this is the lowest that it can get. 

Nothing was going my way anyways and this is the worst that I will have to feel about myself.
I said to myself that I have already hit rock bottom and the only way from is here going to be up!

For that moment on, I gave up thinking about the result and only thought of taking one step at a time in the positive direction.

The only thing I was obsessed with was completing my targets each day, one day at a time. 
When done consistently, I gradually started seeing small changes in my life.

Once I got the momentum rolling, I realized my problems were not bigger than me.

Soon things started turning around and I started getting better at my interviews.
Finally, after a ton of patience and persistence, the day arrived when I received the job offer that changed everything for me.

If you have been laid off, are about to graduate or have already graduated and are looking for a job right now, I completely empathize with you.
The only request I would make to you is understand that your problems are not bigger than you and one soon you’ll be alright.

If I can do it, I truly believe that you can too. Just have FAITH in your process and I promise the right opportunity will be yours soon!

BELIEVE, TRUST and HARD WORK will open doors for you that you never would have imagined existed.
KEEP GOING. In the end, all of us international students are part of the same journey!

P.S: I hope this helps you to keep going forward in your journey. If you faced any similar experiences, let me know about them in the comments below!

--- Post 38 ---
Author: sohansethi
Date: Apr 17, 2026
Engagement: 1,290 reactions, 61 comments, 293 reposts (Score: 7,646)
Media: image
URL: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7444248284681965568

Here's my Ultimate Types of Data Analysis Cheatsheet:

(Save this - every data interview asks about this)

Most people think data analysis is one thing.
It is not.

There are 10 different types and knowing which one to use for which business problem is what separates a junior analyst from a senior one.

Descriptive Analytics
-- What happened?
-- Summarizes past data to track performance and trends
-- Used in dashboards, reports, KPIs

Diagnostic Analytics
-- Why did it happen?
-- Identifies root causes behind trends or issues
-- Drill down, segment data, find correlations

Predictive Analytics
-- What will happen?
-- Forecasts outcomes using data and models
-- Train models, evaluate, predict

Prescriptive Analytics
-- What should we do?
-- Recommends actions using predictions and optimization
-- Guides decision-making

Exploratory Data Analysis (EDA)
-- What patterns exist?
-- Finds patterns, anomalies, and insights
-- Prepares data for analysis

Inferential Analytics
-- Can we generalize this?
-- Applies findings to larger populations
-- Uses hypothesis testing

Real-Time Analytics
-- What’s happening now?
-- Analyzes live data for instant decisions
-- Triggers alerts and dashboards

Qualitative Analytics
-- What do people think?
-- Analyzes text, feedback, and opinions
-- Finds themes and sentiment

Quantitative Analytics
-- What do numbers say?
-- Analyzes numerical data and metrics
-- Uses statistical methods

Causal Analytics
-- Does X cause Y?
-- Identifies cause-effect relationships
-- Uses experiments and validation

Here is what I see most analysts get wrong:

They jump straight to Predictive or Prescriptive analytics before they have mastered Descriptive and Diagnostic.

You cannot forecast what will happen if you do not first understand what already happened and why.

Start with Descriptive. Master Diagnostic. Then move up.

The analysts who get promoted are not the ones who built the most complex models.
They are the ones who picked the right type of analysis for the right business question — every single time.

Which type do you use most in your current role?
♻️ Repost to help someone in your network
💭 Tag a data analyst who needs to see this
📩 Get my full data analytics career guide: https://lnkd.in/gjUqmQ5H

-- Guides decision-making

Exploratory Data Analysis (EDA)
-- What patterns exist?
-- Finds patterns, anomalies, and insights
-- Prepares data for analysis

Inferential Analytics
-- Can we generalize this?
-- Applies findings to larger populations
-- Uses hypothesis testing

Real-Time Analytics
-- What’s happening now?
-- Analyzes live data for instant decisions
-- Triggers alerts and dashboards

Qualitative Analytics
-- What do people think?
-- Analyzes text, feedback, and opinions
-- Finds themes and sentiment

Quantitative Analytics
-- What do numbers say?
-- Analyzes numerical data and metrics
-- Uses statistical methods

Causal Analytics
-- Does X cause Y?
-- Identifies cause-effect relationships
-- Uses experiments and validation

Here is what I see most analysts get wrong:

They jump straight to Predictive or Prescriptive analytics before they have mastered Descriptive and Diagnostic.

You cannot forecast what will happen if you do not first understand what already happened and why.

Start with Descriptive. Master Diagnostic. Then move up.

The analysts who get promoted are not the ones who built the most complex models.
They are the ones who picked the right type of analysis for the right business question — every single time.

Which type do you use most in your current role?
♻️ Repost to help someone in your network

Author: sohansethi
Date: Apr 17, 2026

Media: image
URL: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7449202772123590656

Here's my Ultimate SQL Query Optimization Cheatsheet:
(Save this - slow queries will cost you in production and in interviews)

Writing a query that works is the baseline.
Writing a query that works fast is the skill.

I have seen analysts submit queries that took 45 seconds to load on a dashboard used by 200 people every morning.

That is not a data problem. That is an optimization problem.

Here are 8 techniques that fix it 👇

1. Use Indexes Effectively
Indexes turn slow full table scans into fast direct lookups.

2. Avoid SELECT
Selecting only required columns reduces memory usage and improves query performance.

3. Use EXISTS Instead of IN
EXISTS stops early on match, improving performance for large datasets.

4. Optimize JOINs with Indexed Columns
Indexed join columns prevent repeated scans and significantly speed up joins.

5. Filter Early with WHERE Before GROUP BY
Filtering early reduces rows processed, making aggregations faster and efficient.

6. Avoid Functions on Indexed Columns
Functions on indexed columns disable indexes and force full table scans.

7. Use LIMIT to Reduce Data Load
Limit results to necessary rows to avoid unnecessary data processing overhead.

8. Use Proper Data Types
Matching data types ensures indexes work correctly and avoids hidden performance issues.

Here is the honest truth:

Most analysts never think about query performance until something breaks in production.
By then it is too late.

The analysts who get promoted are the ones who write queries that work for a team of 5 today and still work when 500 people are hitting that dashboard every morning.

Optimization is not advanced SQL.
It is professional SQL.

Which of these mistakes are you still making?
♻️ Repost to help someone level up their SQL
💭 Tag a data analyst who needs to see this

Using this CV will help you get interviews with FAANG:

Your resume is your entry ticket to a job interview,
And with the right resume, you can open some great interview opportunities for yourself.

Here’s exactly how you should structure a good resume:

1. Education:
-- Let the hiring manager know the knowledge that you’ve been trained for.

2. Skills:
-- Convey the skills that you can contribute to the team.

3. Work Experience:
-- Showcase how your previous experience will help you successfully carry out the responsibilities of the role that you’re interviewing for.

4. Projects:
This section helps you portray your expertise in a broad range of skill sets.

5. Leadership and Awards:
Portraying a holistic personality further adds to the credibility of you as a candidate.

Having your resume structured this way will be the easiest way for the hiring manager to select you as a potential candidate and send out an interview invite.

In addition, here are a few tips for a fully optimized resume:

-- If you have less than 10 years of experience, always keep a 1-page resume.
-- Make sure to include tons of metrics and data to support your achievements
-- Always format your bullet like this: Accomplished [X] as measured by [Y], by doing [Z].
-- Tailor a unique resume for each job application to make it ATS-friendly.

If you are currently struggling to get callbacks, try these suggestions.
I assure you, that by implementing them for just 2-3 weeks, you’ll see an increase in the number of callbacks that you get.

This Resume got me interviews at Google, Amazon, JPMorganChase, Capital One, CVS Health, and many more! 
Here’s how you can get them too:

Your resume is your entry ticket to a job interview, and the right structure can open up great opportunities.

Here’s exactly how you should structure a strong resume:

1. Education: Show the knowledge you’ve been trained in.
2. Skills: Highlight the skills you can bring to the team.
3. Work Experience: Show how your past experience helps you handle the role successfully.
4. Projects: Prove expertise across a range of skills.
5. Leadership and Awards: Add credibility and show a more well-rounded profile.

Bonus sections:
6. Summary: A 2–3 line snapshot of who you are professionally.
7. Portfolio: Link your work and let your results speak for themselves.

A resume structured this way makes it easier for hiring managers to see you as a strong candidate and move you toward an interview.

A few quick tips for a stronger resume:
-- If you have less than 10 years of experience, keep it to one page.
-- Use metrics and data to support your achievements.
-- Format your bullet points like this: Accomplished [X] as measured by [Y], by doing [Z].
-- Tailor your resume for each job application to make it more ATS-friendly.

Author: sohansethi
Date: May 17, 2025

URL: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7251788355607113729

From the streets of Mumbai to being featured on Times Square!

I don’t come from a solid financial background
I don’t come from a stellar academic background
I don’t have any influential person rooting for me

Here’s the only thing that worked for me:
Ton’s of Hardwork and, God has been kind!

However, this 10 seconds of fame is filled with 10 years of unsaid sacrifices:

17 yrs: Probably the first time I heard of NYC or Times Square.
17 yrs: I gave up my dream of studying abroad due to financial issues.
18 yrs: I failed to get into a good college.
20 yrs: My 1st startup wasn't generating revenue.
21 yrs: My 2nd startup failed too.
22 yrs: I lost hope, nothing seemed to work out.
22 yrs: I bounced back, working two part-time jobs while attending college.
23 yrs: Got an average job after college, and felt very stagnant.
23 yrs: I decided to give my dream of studying abroad another shot.
24 yrs: Worked relentlessly to build my profile while working full-time.
25 yrs: Finally got into my dream college and came to the USA for MS.
26 yrs: Juggled studies, worked part-time, and applied to 3000+ jobs.
26 yrs: Graduated from UIC with a research assistantship and started working full-time for a great organization.
27 yrs: Built a 30,000-strong LinkedIn community and helped countless international students land full-time jobs.
27 yrs: Finally got featured on Times Square!

It seems that in the last ten years, life has come full circle for me, from 17 to 27.

I am truly grateful for every moment that I felt exhausted, miserable, and defeated. Because that's the only way I could come all this way.

I am grateful to Ankit Agarwal and topmate.io for giving me this prestigious opportunity.
Lastly, thank you to my mom for everything. I am who I am only because of her hard work.

To be given this platform, I want to dedicate this post not just to me, but to anyone reading:

If you are someone who’s currently struggling to make it and still feeling stuck, defeated, or broken, then my message to you is this:
“Believe in yourself, and your dream WILL surely come true!”

I get how you are feeling at this moment, yet this feeling is temporary.
Your best is yet to come, and it's just around the corner for you!

Keep believing, keep striving, keep growing!

P.S: I share daily free resources, tips, and job search prep material. Connect/follow to check them out. I hope this helps, All the best!

I sent 50+ cold messages on LinkedIn… and got ZERO responses.
Then this REFERRAL strategy started getting me responses everyday:

𝐒𝐭𝐞𝐩 𝟏: 𝐎𝐩𝐭𝐢𝐦𝐢𝐳𝐞 𝐘𝐨𝐮𝐫 𝐏𝐫𝐨𝐟𝐢𝐥𝐞 
✅ Add the "Open to Work" banner
✅ Update your skills & certifications
✅ Write a compelling headline
(Not just “Job Seeker,” but "Data Analyst | SQL, Python, Power BI | Turning Data into Insights")

𝐒𝐭𝐞𝐩 𝟐: 𝐄𝐧𝐠𝐚𝐠𝐞 𝐰𝐢𝐭𝐡 𝐓𝐚𝐫𝐠𝐞𝐭 𝐂𝐨𝐦𝐩𝐚𝐧𝐢𝐞𝐬 
✅ Follow employees from your target companies
✅ Connect with recruiters & hiring managers
✅ Comment on their posts to get noticed
✅ Share relevant content to show your expertise

𝐒𝐭𝐞𝐩 𝟑: 𝐂𝐫𝐚𝐟𝐭 𝐘𝐨𝐮𝐫 𝐌𝐞𝐬𝐬𝐚𝐠𝐞
✅ Keep it short & specific
✅ Mention the exact role you’re applying for
✅ Show company knowledge (Not just “I love Tesla” – be specific!)
✅ Request a quick chat, NOT just a referral

BONUS: My Referral Message Template (Feel free to steal this 👇)

“Hello Elon, 
I hope you are doing well.

I am reaching out to request a referral and possible guidance to become a part of an esteemed organization such as Tesla.

I have 4+ years of experience in Business Analytics and I specialize in skills such as SQL, Power BI, Tableau, and Python.

If someone like you who has already walked the path that I am setting on can guide me, it would be a big help to learn how I can best leverage my skills to contribute to Tesla's future growth.

I am interested in the Data Scientist role at Tesla. 
Job ID: #123456

Let me know if you would be open to assisting with the referral and any additional information that would be required from my end.

Thank you for considering my request, it means a lot!

Best Regards, 
Sohan”

P.S: Elon here is a Manager/Director, not the CEO of Tesla, haha!

What NOT to Do When Asking for Referrals:
❌ Don't spam connections (Sending 50+ messages won’t help—sending 5 well-crafted ones will)
❌ Don't use generic messages ("Can you refer me?" is an instant turn-off)
❌ Don't ask immediately (Engage first, then request)
❌ Don't be pushy (Respect their time)
❌ Don't forget to follow up (A polite check-in can make all the difference)

These strategies were personally super beneficial during my job search.
Referrals can 10X your job search success. But only if you do it the right way!

--- Post 55 ---
Author: sohansethi
Date: Mar 18, 2026
Engagement: 897 reactions, 31 comments, 127 reposts (Score: 5,049)
Media: image
URL: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7438955755740999680

"Data Analytics" & "Data Analysis" are not the same thing.

Here is what actually separates them:

1. Programming → Analysis uses Python, R, Scala at an advanced level. Analytics uses SQL, basic Python, Excel formulas.

2. Data Sources → Analysis works with Snowflake, BigQuery, Data Lakes. Analytics works with MySQL, CSVs, internal business databases.

3. Processing → Analysis uses Spark, Databricks, dbt, Airflow. Analytics uses Power Query, Pandas, scheduled SQL queries.

4. Statistics → Analysis does ML models, predictive analytics, time-series forecasting. Analytics does descriptive stats, hypothesis testing, basic regression.

5. Visualization → Analysis builds in Looker, Plotly, Superset. Analytics builds in Power BI, Tableau, Google Data Studio.

6. Cloud → Analysis uses AWS, GCP, Azure infrastructure. Analytics uses cloud BI dashboards and Power BI service.

7. Final Output → Analysis produces predictive models, automated pipelines, ML-driven insights. Analytics produces dashboards, business reports, KPI tracking.

The simplest way I explain it:
- Data Analysis answers what happened and why.
- Data Analytics answers what will happen next and what we should do about it.

If you are early in your career, start with Analytics. Learn SQL, Excel, Power BI, basic stats, and how to communicate findings to non-technical people. 

Analysis roles - pipelines, ML models, cloud infrastructure - come after that foundation is solid.

Know which lane you are in. Then go deep.

5 things I wish I had known before coming to the US:

1. Landing in the US is just the start of your struggles. 
Things don't get easy, they only get tougher and complicated for the next few years.

2. Let alone America, currently no country is the land of opportunities.
Getting a job as an international student will be the hardest thing you will ever go through.

3. Education loans will become a burden you can't get easily rid of.
Low salaries and high interest rates can make you feel lost in a pool of debt.

4. There will come days that will be the loneliest time of your lives.
Depression, anxiety, and loneliness are unsaid realities of the lives of some international students.

5. Life will be very unpredictable and you'll feel lost between two worlds.
Layoffs and visa issues make the future very unclear and it may feel like the struggle never ends.

However, on the other side life in the US will introduce you to a diverse community, a beautiful country, meaningful experiences, and a journey of self-transformation, where you get an opportunity to build a life of freedom and prosperity.

What are your thoughts? Let me know in the comments below.

Author: sohansethi
Date: May 17, 2025
Engagement: 814 reactions, 62 comments, 52 reposts (Score: 4,794)
Media: image
URL: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7317539674531475458

I am hiring you as a Data Analyst:
(If you can answer these 30 SQL questions)

A. WINDOWS FUNCTIONS:
1. Calculate the cumulative sum of sales for each region.
2. Rank products by their sales within each category.
3. Determine a running total of expenses by transaction date.
4. Identify the top 5 performing sales representatives by quarter.
5. Compute the year-over-year difference in revenue for each store.

B. COMMON TABLE EXPRESSIONS(CTE):
1. Write a recursive CTE to calculate Fibonacci numbers.
2. Use a CTE to identify the reporting hierarchy for a given employee.
3. Flatten a tree-structured data model using a CTE.
4. Use a recursive CTE to find the path between two nodes in a graph.
5. Analyze monthly sales trends using CTEs for better readability.

C. JOINS (INNER, OUTER, CROSS, SELF):
1. List customers who have purchased a specific product (Inner Join).
2. Identify orders that have no assigned delivery agent (Left Join).
3. Get products that were never sold (Right Join).
4. Generate all possible combinations of stores and their products (Cross Join).
5. Pair employees within the same team for mentorship opportunities (Self Join).

D. SUBQUERIES:
1. Find products priced above the average price in their category.
2. Identify employees who earn more than the average salary of their peers.
3. List customers who placed orders in every month of the year.
4. Retrieve the second and third highest salaries using subqueries.
5. Determine regions where no orders have been placed.

E. AGGREGATE FUNCTIONS:
1. Calculate the total sales for each product category.
2. Find the average number of orders per customer.
3. Identify the maximum, minimum, and average revenue generated by stores.
4. Count the number of active customers in each region.
5. Compute the variance and standard deviation of product prices.

F. INDEXING AND QUERY OPTIMIZATION:
1. Identify slow-running queries that could benefit from indexing.
2. Analyze the execution plan to optimize performance.
3. Compare query performance before and after adding an index.
4. Write a query to find unused indexes in a database.
5. Optimize a complex join query for faster execution.

This Cold-Message Template got me 7 Referrals in 1 week:

Most job seekers approach referrals completely wrong. 
They send generic "Can you refer me?" messages to strangers and wonder why they get ignored.

Here's the 3-step system that actually works:

Step 1: Optimize Your Profile:

→ Add the "Open to Work" banner
→ Update your skills & certifications
→ Write a compelling headline
(Not just “Job Seeker,” but "Data Analyst | SQL, Python, Power BI | Turning Data into Insights")

Step 2: Find the RIGHT People:

Search: "[Job Title] AND hiring" in quotes 
→ Filter by Posts 
→ Use "All Filters" for specific companies
→ Follow employees from your target companies 
→ Target employees with 2+ years of experience

Step 3: Craft Your Message:

→ Keep it short & specific
→ Mention the exact role you’re applying for
→ Show company knowledge (Not just “I love Tesla” – be specific!)
→ Request a quick chat, NOT just a referral

BONUS: My Referral Message Template (Feel free to steal this 👇)

“Hello Elon,
I hope you are doing well.

I am reaching out to request a referral and possible guidance to become a part of an esteemed organization such as Tesla.

I have 4+ years of experience in Business Analytics and I specialize in skills such as SQL, Power BI, Tableau, and Python.

If someone like you who has already walked the path that I am setting on can guide me, it would be a big help to learn how I can best leverage my skills to contribute to Tesla's future growth.

I am interested in the Data Scientist role at Tesla.
Job ID: #123456

Let me know if you would be open to assisting with the referral and any additional information that would be required from my end.

Thank you for considering my request, it means a lot!

Best Regards,
Sohan”

P.S: Elon here is a Manager/Director, not the CEO of Tesla, haha!

Link: https://lnkd.in/g3neHw2Y

→ Try Apollo.io for contact discovery 
→ Use Hunter.io for email verification

Step 2: Do Your Homework 

→ Research their recent LinkedIn activity 
→ Find common connections or interests
→ Identify their role in decision-making
→ Google "[Company] + news" for recent wins 

Step 3: Write emails that gets you a response 

→ Start with a genuine personalization 
→ Lead with value, not your needs 
→ Include one specific ask 
→ End with an easy a CTA

Loved your recent post about streamlining customer onboarding at Amazon. The 40% efficiency gain caught my attention.

I've helped 3 SaaS companies reduce their onboarding time by 50%+ using automated workflow tools.

Would a 10-minute call next week be worth exploring how this could work for Amazon?

Best,
Sohan"

Tip 1: Keep it short, crisp and valuable for the reader

BONUS: You can ace your next interview by preparing using Final Round AI.

I hope this helps you to settle and navigate your way smoothly:

1. Top creators to follow for your journey abroad:
a. Pritesh Jagani
b. Ritika Saraswat
c. Jahnavi Shah
d. Supreet Kaur
e. Soundarya Balasubramani
f. Varun Negandhi
g. Farhoon Asim

Their advice personally helped me navigate a ton of challenges from time to time in the last 3 years.

2. Job search portals for Internships and Full-time jobs:
a. Handshake - https://lnkd.in/eJkwtXpS
b. Indeed - www.indeed.com
c. BuiltIn - www.builtin.com

These are the 3 main websites apart from LinkedIn that I heavily relied on for my job search.

3. Websites to find jobs with H1-B sponsorship:
a. https://h1bgrader.com/
b. https://h1bdata.info/

Jobs for international students are limited. It is better to research and align with opportunities that offer sponsorship.

Attending events through this platform helped me network with like-minded individuals and get leads and referrals during my job search process.

--- Post 71 ---
Author: sohansethi
Date: Apr 17, 2026
Engagement: 724 reactions, 60 comments, 117 reposts (Score: 4,454)
Media: image
URL: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7447345793042214912

Data Analyst vs Business Analyst

Two of the most confused job titles in tech.

I get asked about this every week. So here is the clearest breakdown I can give — from someone who has hired both.

Programming & Tools
-- BA: Excel advanced formulas, SQL basic queries, PowerPoint, VBA
-- DA: SQL, Python (Pandas, NumPy), R, Excel advanced

Data Sources & Systems
-- BA: CRM systems, ERP systems, Excel datasets, internal business tools
-- DA: Data warehouses, data lakes, structured and unstructured datasets, large-scale databases

Data Processing & Transformation
-- BA: Excel transformations, manual data cleaning, Power Query, basic SQL filtering
-- DA: SQL transformations, ETL/ELT pipelines, data cleaning and wrangling, dbt, Python processing

Statistical Analysis & Modeling
-- BA: Trend analysis, basic statistical summaries, KPI comparisons, scenario analysis
-- DA: Descriptive statistics, predictive modeling, regression analysis, time-series forecasting

Data Visualization Tools
-- BA: Excel charts, Power BI dashboards, Google Sheets, PowerPoint reports
-- DA: Tableau, Power BI, Looker, Plotly, Python dashboards

Data Warehousing & Platforms
-- BA: Company BI tools, Excel reports, CRM dashboards, internal reporting systems
-- DA: Snowflake, BigQuery, Redshift, Databricks

Automation & Workflow
-- BA: Excel automation, Power BI refresh, manual reporting, basic macros
-- DA: Airflow, dbt, Python scripts, scheduled SQL jobs

Cloud Platforms
-- BA: Cloud BI dashboards, Google Sheets, Power BI Service, Tableau Cloud
-- DA: AWS, GCP, Azure infrastructure

Monitoring & Data Quality
-- BA: Manual validation, Excel checks, dashboard monitoring, business rule validation
-- DA: Great Expectations, Monte Carlo, Datafold, SQL validation checks

Final Output
-- BA: Business reports, KPI dashboards, insights for decisions, stakeholder presentations
-- DA: Data models, predictive insights, automated dashboards, data pipelines

Here is the simplest way I explain it:

– A Business Analyst translates business problems into data questions.
– A Data Analyst translates data into business answers.

Both roles are valuable.
Both require completely different skill sets.

The mistake I see constantly - candidates applying for DA roles with a BA skill set and wondering why they keep getting rejected.

Know which role you are actually qualified for.

Then build the skills that role actually requires.

Which one are you currently targeting?

♻️ Repost - this clears up a confusion most people have
💭 Tag someone trying to decide between these two paths
📩 Get my full data analytics career guide: https://lnkd.in/gjUqmQ5H

============================================================

--- Post 74 ---
Author: sohansethi
Date: Aug 20, 2025
Engagement: 748 reactions, 42 comments, 5 reposts (Score: 4,170)
Media: image
URL: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7359219431488540672

My LinkedIn profile says I'm working as a Data Analytics Manager in the USA and living my dream life,
Yet I will say, It’s all a LIE!

Because, my LinkedIn doesn’t say that:
-- There was a time when I was jobless and had more than $50,000 in student loan debt.

-- There was a time when I missed major festivals, friend’s marriages, and parents’ birthdays back home.

-- There was a time when I was wiping tables and cleaning dishes as a part-time student.

-- There was a time when I felt out of place and had difficulty making new friends in a foreign country.

-- There was a time when I was stressed for months and faced endless rejections during my job search period.

Life as an international student is at times full of hard work, anxiety and unexpected struggles.

Thing's might seem sorted right now, however, just a year ago my life was filled with uncertainty.
I was about to graduate, didn't have a job in hand and was thinking how will I pay my bills from the next few months.

The fear of paying rent, managing living expenses and preparing for unexpected circumstances was definitely tough.
Moreover, having a feeling of not being good enough while at the same time seeing everybody else do better than me was surely the most anxious thing of all.

However, while I was feeling all these emotions, I realized this is the lowest that it can get. 

Nothing was going my way anyways and this is the worst that I will have to feel about myself.
I said to myself that I have already hit rock bottom and the only way from is here going to be up!

For that moment on, I gave up thinking about the result and only thought of taking one step at a time in the positive direction.

The only thing I was obsessed with was completing my targets each day, one day at a time. 
When done consistently, I gradually started seeing small changes in my life.

Once I got the momentum rolling, I realized my problems were not bigger than me.

Soon things started turning around and I started getting better at my interviews.
Finally, after a ton of patience and persistence, the day arrived when I received the job offer that changed everything for me.

If you have been laid off, are about to graduate or have already graduated and are looking for a job right now, I completely empathize with you.
The only request I would make to you is understand that your problems are not bigger than you and one soon you’ll be alright.

If I can do it, I truly believe that you can too. Just have FAITH in your process and I promise the right opportunity will be yours soon!

BELIEVE, TRUST and HARD WORK will open doors for you that you never would have imagined existed.
KEEP GOING. In the end, all of us international students are part of the same journey!

P.S: I hope this helps you to keep going forward in your journey. If you faced any similar experiences, let me know about them in the comments below!

”I realized the only person who could change my story was me”

-- Treating my job search like a full-time job 
-- Building genuine connections instead of just networking 
-- Learning from each rejection instead of getting defeated 
-- Upgrading my skills even when it felt pointless

Constantly working on myself has been the key for me!

Today, I am grateful for: 
-- Leading projects I once dreamed about 
-- Mentoring others on their journey abroad 
-- Building a community of 60,000+ international students 
-- Living proof that if you dream big, your dreams can come true!

The Truth About Success: 
It's not about never falling. 
It's about rising one more time when you fall.

3. Your breakthrough might be just one step away

2 Bags, 3 Months of Rent and $50,000 in Debt,
That's all I brought to the USA with me 3 years ago

Before I knew it, every day was more of a survival and less of a glamorous experience abroad.

The 1st one year was filled with:
— 15-hour days juggled between part-time jobs and studies
— Cleaning tables and wiping floors to earn $13/hr
— Virtual birthdays, weddings, and missing your loved ones back home
— Exhausting job-search process with 3000+ applications (and 99% rejections)

Soon I was totally exhausted, anxious and alone.
Yet, something deep inside still wanted me to keep going!

And, things soon changed near my graduation:
— I started to get my confidence back
— Got my dream job in a great company
— Built a 95,000+ community on LinkedIn (this I am most grateful for)
— Featured on a billboard at Times Square in NYC

Now in hindsight, I can proudly say that coming to the USA with just 2 Bags and 3 Months of Rent was the best decision ever!

Having said that, this post is not about me. My aim with this story is to convey an important message to the reader (To you, yes you beautiful human reading this)

My Message is this: 
In my darkest days, feeling lost and defeated, I never imagined I'd build this platform that I am grateful for today. 

It was only those struggles that shaped me and made it happen. 

If you’re feeling stuck now, remember that every setback is a setup for an incredible future. Life’s toughest seasons often lead to the most beautiful breakthroughs. 

If I could do this, I have even more faith in you.
Your story will indeed be a remarkable one!

Take this post as a reminder that:
1. You are bigger than all your problems.
2. You are special in your own beautiful way.
3. Everything you are going through will soon be worth it!

This Resume got me interviews at Google, Amazon, JPMorganChase, Capital One, CVS Health and many more!
Here's how you can get them too:

Your resume is your entry ticket to a job interview,
And with the right resume, you can open some great interview opportunities for yourself.

Here’s exactly how you should structure a good resume:

1. Education:
-- Let the hiring manager know the knowledge that you’ve been trained for.

2. Skills:
-- Convey the skills that you can contribute to the team.

3. Work Experience:
-- Showcase how your previous experience will help you successfully carry out the responsibilities of the role that you’re interviewing for.

4. Projects:
This section helps you portray your expertise in a broad range of skill sets.

5. Leadership and Awards:
Portraying a holistic personality further adds to the credibility of you as a candidate.

Having your resume structured this way will be the easiest way for the hiring manager to select you as a potential candidate and send out an interview invite.

In addition, here are a few tips for a fully optimized resume:

-- If you have less than 10 years of experience, always keep a 1-page resume.
-- Make sure to include tons of metrics and data to support your achievements
-- Always format your bullet like this: Accomplished [X] as measured by [Y], by doing [Z].
-- Tailor a unique resume for each job application to make it ATS-friendly.

If you are currently struggling to get callbacks, try these suggestions.
I assure you, that by implementing them for just 2-3 weeks, you’ll see an increase in the number of callbacks that you get.

𝗛𝗲𝗿𝗲'𝘀 𝗺𝘆 𝗨𝗹𝘁𝗶𝗺𝗮𝘁𝗲 𝗗𝗮𝘁𝗮 𝗔𝗻𝗮𝗹𝘆𝘁𝗶𝗰𝘀 𝗖𝗵𝗲𝗮𝘁 𝗦𝗵𝗲𝗲𝘁:
(Save this — everything you need in one place)

Most people learning data analytics are overwhelmed.
Too many tools. Too many courses. Too many opinions on where to start.

This cheat sheet cuts through all of it 👇

𝗧𝗵𝗲 𝗟𝗲𝗮𝗿𝗻𝗶𝗻𝗴 𝗥𝗼𝗮𝗱𝗺𝗮𝗽
1. Intro to Data Analytics - what it is, types, and real world use cases
2. Foundational Concepts - data types, lifecycle, basic statistics
3. Excel - formulas, pivot tables, dashboard basics
4. SQL - SELECT, WHERE, GROUP BY, JOINs, window functions
5. Python - Pandas, NumPy, data cleaning, EDA, visualization
6. Data Visualization - chart selection, storytelling, Power BI, Tableau
7. Statistics - hypothesis testing, correlation, regression basics
8. Business Understanding - KPIs, stakeholder communication, decision-making


𝗗𝗮𝘁𝗮 𝗔𝗻𝗮𝗹𝘆𝘁𝗶𝗰𝘀 𝗶𝗻 𝗮 𝗡𝘂𝘁𝘀𝗵𝗲𝗹𝗹
-- Data Collection → Raw data from databases, APIs, and business systems
-- Data Cleaning → Handling missing values, duplicates, inconsistencies
-- Data Processing → Transforming raw data into structured usable formats
-- Analysis → Applying SQL, statistics, and logic to extract insights
-- Visualization → Charts and dashboards to communicate findings
-- Decision Making → Turning insights into actionable business decisions

𝗞𝗲𝘆 𝗖𝗼𝗻𝗰𝗲𝗽𝘁𝘀 𝗘𝘃𝗲𝗿𝘆 𝗔𝗻𝗮𝗹𝘆𝘀𝘁 𝗠𝘂𝘀𝘁 𝗞𝗻𝗼𝘄
-- EDA → Uncovering patterns, trends, and anomalies in datasets
-- KPI → Metrics used to measure business performance
-- A/B Testing → Comparing two variations to determine better performance
-- Data Pipeline → System that collects, processes, and stores data
-- Automation → Using scripts to reduce manual data tasks

𝗙𝗿𝗲𝗲 𝗬𝗼𝘂𝗧𝘂𝗯𝗲 𝗖𝗵𝗮𝗻𝗻𝗲𝗹𝘀
-- Alex The Analyst, Luke Barousse, Ken Jee, StatQuest, Krish Naik

𝗧𝗼𝗽 𝗪𝗲𝗯𝘀𝗶𝘁𝗲𝘀 𝘁𝗼 𝗣𝗿𝗮𝗰𝘁𝗶𝗰𝗲
-- kaggle.com, w3schools.com/sql, mode.com/sql-tutorial, geeksforgeeks.org

𝗙𝗿𝗲𝗲 𝗗𝗮𝘁𝗮𝘀𝗲𝘁𝘀
-- Kaggle, Google Dataset Search, UCI ML Repository

The roadmap exists.
The resources are free.
The only variable is whether you actually start.
Where are you on this roadmap right now?

--- Post 82 ---
Author: sohansethi
Date: May 17, 2025
Engagement: 675 reactions, 36 comments, 23 reposts (Score: 3,781)
Media: image
URL: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7252697754458599425

This is a famous interview question from Goldman Sachs:

Question: Goldman Sachs has various products and they want to analyse their sales of each product. They asked you to write a SQL query that calculates the running total of sales for each product, ordered by the month of the year.

You can find full details here:: https://lnkd.in/gpVPHynu

Here's my step-by-step explanation:

Step 1: Extract the Month

We use the EXTRACT(MONTH FROM sale_date) function to pull out the month from the sale_date. This will allow us to display the sales totals by month.

Step 2: Running Total Calculation

The core part of the solution is using the SUM() OVER() window function.

We calculate the running total using SUM(sale_amount) while partitioning by product_id. This ensures that each product's sales are summed individually.

Step 3: Order the results

The ORDER BY inside the window function ensures that the sum is computed in the correct order, based on the month the sales occurred.

Step 4: Final Output

The query outputs the month, product_id, and the computed running_total for each product, ordered by both month and product for readability.

This is a semi-medium level problem and should take about 5-10 minutes to get to the solution.

I hope this helps you ace your next SQL interview.

Resume tips shared by a Google Recruiter:

(Receive more callbacks using these simple fixes)

Over 98% Resume's get rejected by the ATS due to:
• Over-designed templates
• Incorrect section structure
• Missing or misplaced keywords
• Unreadable formatting for ATS software

Here’s exactly how you should structure a good resume:

1. Summary:
-- Write a short 2-3 line objective to position yourself and display your value.

2. Education:
-- Let the hiring manager know the knowledge that you’ve been trained for.

3. Skills:
-- Convey the skills that you can contribute to the team.

4. Work Experience:
-- Showcase how your previous experience will help you successfully carry out the responsibilities of the role that you’re interviewing for.

5. Projects:
-- This section helps you portray your expertise in a broad range of skill sets.

6. Extra-Curricular & Leadership:
-- Portraying a holistic personality further adds to the credibility of you as a candidate.

Having your resume structured this way will be the easiest way for the hiring manager to select you as a potential candidate and send out an interview invite.

In addition, here are a few tips for a fully optimized resume:

-- If you have less than 10 years of experience, always keep a 1-page resume.
-- Make sure to include tons of metrics and data to support your achievements
-- Always format your bullet like this: Accomplished [X] as measured by [Y], by doing [Z].
-- Tailor a unique resume for each job application to make it ATS-friendly.

If you are currently struggling to get callbacks, try these suggestions.
I assure you, that by implementing them for just 2-3 weeks, you’ll see an increase in the number of callbacks that you get.

My aim with this post is to help everyone currently in the job market to excel in their job search process.

--- Post 89 ---
Author: sohansethi
Date: May 17, 2025
Engagement: 650 reactions, 40 comments, 9 reposts (Score: 3,668)
Media: image
URL: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7320086105637212161

I sent 50+ cold messages on LinkedIn… and got ZERO responses.
Then this REFERRAL strategy started getting me responses everyday:

𝐒𝐭𝐞𝐩 𝟏: 𝐎𝐩𝐭𝐢𝐦𝐢𝐳𝐞 𝐘𝐨𝐮𝐫 𝐏𝐫𝐨𝐟𝐢𝐥𝐞 
✅ Add the "Open to Work" banner
✅ Update your skills & certifications
✅ Write a compelling headline
(Not just “Job Seeker,” but "Data Analyst | SQL, Python, Power BI | Turning Data into Insights")

𝐒𝐭𝐞𝐩 𝟐: 𝐄𝐧𝐠𝐚𝐠𝐞 𝐰𝐢𝐭𝐡 𝐓𝐚𝐫𝐠𝐞𝐭 𝐂𝐨𝐦𝐩𝐚𝐧𝐢𝐞𝐬 
✅ Follow employees from your target companies
✅ Connect with recruiters & hiring managers
✅ Comment on their posts to get noticed
✅ Share relevant content to show your expertise

𝐒𝐭𝐞𝐩 𝟑: 𝐂𝐫𝐚𝐟𝐭 𝐘𝐨𝐮𝐫 𝐌𝐞𝐬𝐬𝐚𝐠𝐞
✅ Keep it short & specific
✅ Mention the exact role you’re applying for
✅ Show company knowledge (Not just “I love Tesla” – be specific!)
✅ Request a quick chat, NOT just a referral

BONUS: My Referral Message Template (Feel free to steal this 👇)

“Hello Elon, 
I hope you are doing well.

I am reaching out to request a referral and possible guidance to become a part of an esteemed organization such as Tesla.

I have 4+ years of experience in Business Analytics and I specialize in skills such as SQL, Power BI, Tableau, and Python.

If someone like you who has already walked the path that I am setting on can guide me, it would be a big help to learn how I can best leverage my skills to contribute to Tesla's future growth.

I am interested in the Data Scientist role at Tesla. 
Job ID: #123456

Let me know if you would be open to assisting with the referral and any additional information that would be required from my end.

Thank you for considering my request, it means a lot!

Best Regards, 
Sohan”

P.S: Elon here is a Manager/Director, not the CEO of Tesla, haha!

What NOT to Do When Asking for Referrals:
❌ Don't spam connections (Sending 50+ messages won’t help—sending 5 well-crafted ones will)
❌ Don't use generic messages ("Can you refer me?" is an instant turn-off)
❌ Don't ask immediately (Engage first, then request)
❌ Don't be pushy (Respect their time)
❌ Don't forget to follow up (A polite check-in can make all the difference)

These strategies were personally super beneficial during my job search.
Referrals can 10X your job search success. But only if you do it the right way!

💭 Comment 
♻️ Repost

--- Post 92 ---
Author: sohansethi
Date: Mar 18, 2026
Engagement: 675 reactions, 20 comments, 13 reposts (Score: 3,601)
Media: image
URL: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7436794751447871488

This Resume got me interviews at Google, Amazon, JPMorganChase, Capital One, CVS Health and many more!
Here's how you can get them too:

Your resume is your entry ticket to a job interview,
And with the right resume, you can open some great interview opportunities for yourself.

Here’s exactly how you should structure a good resume:

1. Education:
-- Let the hiring manager know the knowledge that you’ve been trained for.

2. Skills:
-- Convey the skills that you can contribute to the team.

3. Work Experience:
-- Showcase how your previous experience will help you successfully carry out the responsibilities of the role that you’re interviewing for.

4. Projects:
This section helps you portray your expertise in a broad range of skill sets.

5. Leadership and Awards:
Portraying a holistic personality further adds to the credibility of you as a candidate.

Having your resume structured this way will be the easiest way for the hiring manager to select you as a potential candidate and send out an interview invite.

In addition, here are a few tips for a fully optimized resume:

-- If you have less than 10 years of experience, always keep a 1-page resume.
-- Make sure to include tons of metrics and data to support your achievements
-- Always format your bullet like this: Accomplished [X] as measured by [Y], by doing [Z].
-- Tailor a unique resume for each job application to make it ATS-friendly.

If you are currently struggling to get callbacks, try these suggestions.
I assure you, that by implementing them for just 2-3 weeks, you’ll see an increase in the number of callbacks that you get.

My aim with this post is to help everyone currently in the job market to excel in their job search process.

I am proud to announce I returned to UIC - my master's college.
Not as a struggling student this time,
But as a speaker helping job seekers navigate their job search:

3 years ago at UIC: 
→ I took an education loan of $50,000+ to fund my masters 
→ Worked 3-4hrs part-time daily while maintaining a 4/4 GPA
→ Got rejected 3000+ times in 9 months 
→ Questioned if my degree and efforts were even worth it

Last week at UIC: 
→ Shared tips that actually worked in my job search 
→ Helped 60+ students build their Resume's and LinkedIn portfolio's.
→ Shared strategies that actually bring results

Here's what I told them (and wish someone told me 3 years ago):
-- Quality > Quantity: Stop mass applying.
Send 15 tailored applications instead of 100 generic ones.

-- Network Like a Human: Don't ask for jobs, Ask for advice. 
Opportunities will follow naturally.

-- Use Rejection as Data: Each "no" tells you what to improve. 
Optimize feedback to get 1% better daily.

-- Your Timeline ≠ Others' Timeline
 I took 9 months. Some take 3. Some take 18. All are valid.

My message to every job seeker reading this:

Your struggle is temporary. Your resilience is permanent. 
Your success story is being written right now.

A big thank you to Re-Defined and INFORMS at University of Illinois, Chicago for helping me live this moment. 

This was only possible because of each one of your support:
Bhavya Passi, Ritika Saraswat, Mohammed Wasim, Shree Parida

For everyone still in their struggle phase:
One day, you'll return to the place that broke you. 
Not to relive the pain. But to help others survive it.

Your full-circle moment is coming.
Keep applying. Keep learning. Keep believing!

At 17 yrs: I had to abandon my dream of studying abroad due to monetary issues.
At 18 yrs: I failed to get into a good college.
At 20 yrs: My 1st startup wasn't generating revenue.
At 21 yrs: My 2nd startup failed too.
At 22 yrs: I lost hope, nothing seemed to work out.
At 22 yrs: I pulled myself back up and worked 2 part-time jobs along with college.
At 23 yrs: Got an average job after college, and felt very stagnant.
At 23 yrs: I decided to give my dream of studying abroad another shot.
At 24 yrs: Worked relentlessly to build my profile while working full-time.
At 25 yrs: Finally got into my dream college and came to the USA for a master's.
At 26 yrs: Juggled studies, worked part-time, and applied to 3000+ full-time jobs.
At 26 yrs: Graduated from UIC with a research assistantship and started working full-time for a great organization.
At 27 yrs: Built a 20,000-strong LinkedIn community that I am immensely grateful for and helped countless international students land full-time jobs.

It seems like from 17 to 27, life has come full circle for me in the last 10 years.

I am truly grateful for every moment that I felt exhausted, miserable, and defeated. Because that's the only way I could come all this way.

If you are at the downside of a disadvantage and it seems like nothing in life is working out, always remember:
"Believe, and it will happen!"

I get how you are feeling at this moment, yet this feeling is temporary. Your best is yet to come, and it's just around the corner for you!

Keep believing, keep striving, keep growing!

At 26 yrs: Juggled studies, worked part-time, and applied to 3000+ full-time jobs.
At 26 yrs: Graduated from UIC with a research assistantship and started working full-time for a great organization.
At 27 yrs: Built a 20,000-strong LinkedIn community that I am immensely grateful for and helped countless international students land full-time jobs.

It seems like from 17 to 27, life has come full circle for me in the last 10 years.

I am truly grateful for every moment that I felt exhausted, miserable, and defeated. Because that's the only way I could come all this way.

If you are at the downside of a disadvantage and it seems like nothing in life is working out, always remember:
"Believe, and it will happen!"

I get how you are feeling at this moment, yet this feeling is temporary. Your best is yet to come, and it's just around the corner for you!

Keep believing, keep striving, keep growing!

I put everything on the line to come to USA:

Quit my stable job. Took $50,000 in debt. Left everyone I loved behind.

Everyone said I am making a huge mistake.

I gave up my: 
→ Comfortable salary 
→ Comfort of Family 
→ Financial security 
→ Peace of mind

What I got instead:
→ Pure survival mode 
→ Cleaning dishes at $13/hour 
→ Sleeping 3-4 hours daily 
→ Applying to 200+ jobs weekly 
→ Eating $2 meals to save money 
→ Missing every celebration back home

My lowest point was having $50,000 in debt and 0 Job offers after 3,000+ applications.

I sat in my university library one day and questioned everything.
"Did I destroy my life for nothing?"

However, at this exact moment, something magical happened:
-- I Stopped waiting for perfect opportunities. 
-- I Started creating them relentlessly

→ 150 applications per week 
→ 30+ cold messages daily
→ Ready to unpaid work for experience 
→ Treating job search like my job

Than within 3 weeks came the email that changed everything.
"We're pleased to offer you a job as a Business Analyst..."

Finally, my journey made sense!

3 years later: 
→ Debt: Cleared 
→ Job: Data Analytics Manager
→ Community: 100,000+ on LinkedIn 
→ Featured on: Times Square billboard
→ Featured in: Business Insider, CNBC, Fox News
→ Fulfilled my dream: Gave a TEDx speech
→ Impact: Helping thousands of students succeed 

The truth about putting everything on the line:
Yes, it's terrifying. Yes, you'll question it daily. Yes, you might fail.

But playing it safe guarantees you'll never discover what you're truly capable of.

My biggest risk became my biggest reward.

For everyone betting everything on their dreams - your struggle is temporary, your growth will be permanent. 

You've got this, Keep going!

The HARSH TRUTH Of Job Search No One is Talking About in 2025:

Here’s what career "Experts" say: 
— "Just network more!" 
— "Customize every application!" 
— "Follow up with HR’s persistently!" 
— "Your resume needs more keywords!" 
— "Just be yourself in interviews!"

However, here’s The BRUTAL reality job seekers face: 
— 200+ applications with zero response 
— Ghosted after 5 rounds of interviews 
— Rejection emails with zero feedback 
— AI resume filters rejecting qualified candidates 
— "Entry-level" roles requiring 3+ years experience, haha!

Here’s why I feel the system is BROKEN:
1. HR is overwhelmed with applications
2. Hiring managers are drowning in endless interviews
3. Job descriptions rarely match actual needs
4. Internal politics can affect final decisions
5. Luck plays a bigger role than anything else!

What ACTUALLY works in 2025: 
— Building genuine relationships before you need them 
— Getting internal referrals (the secret backdoor) 
— Creating valuable projects that demonstrate skills 
— Focusing on solving organization’s problems 
— Understanding that rejections are rarely personal

Remember this: 
1. Your worth isn't determined by your job status. 
2. Your skills don't disappear during unemployment. 
3. Your journey isn't going to be the same as anyone else's.

Most importantly: It only takes ONE yes❤️
The right opportunity is coming your way soon!

Author: sohansethi
Date: Apr 19, 2026

Media: image
URL: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7451272136893038592

Here's the Complete Data Analytics Tools Ecosystem for 2026:
(Save this - every tool you need to know in one place)

One of the most common questions I get:
"Which tools should I actually learn for data analytics?"

The honest answer - it depends on the role you are targeting.
Here is the full breakdown 👇

Programming & Core Analysis
-- Python → Data cleaning, analysis, automation with Pandas and NumPy
-- R → Statistical analysis and advanced visualizations

Databases & Query Engines
-- MySQL / PostgreSQL → Store structured data and run SQL queries
-- Snowflake / BigQuery → Cloud data warehouses for large-scale analytics

Data Transformation & Processing
-- dbt → Transform raw data into analytics-ready datasets
-- Apache Spark → Large-scale distributed data processing

Data Engineering & Pipelines
-- Apache Airflow → Schedule and orchestrate data workflows
-- Apache Kafka → Real-time data streaming and ingestion

Data Visualization & BI
-- Tableau → Interactive dashboards and insights
-- Power BI → Business reporting and enterprise dashboards

Spreadsheets & Lightweight Analytics
-- Excel → Data analysis, formulas, pivot tables
-- Google Sheets → Collaborative data analysis and sharing

Development & Analysis Environment
-- Jupyter Notebook → Code, visualization, and documentation in one place

AI-Powered Analytics (2026 Shift)
-- ChatGPT / AI Copilots → Data cleaning, querying, and insight generation

Data Quality & Testing
-- Great Expectations → Data validation and testing

Version Control & Collaboration
-- Git / GitHub → Version control for data projects

Here is the honest truth:
You do not need to know all of these.

For your first data analyst role you need:
SQL + Python + one BI tool + Excel + Git

Master those first.
Add tools based on what your team actually uses.

The analysts who chase every new tool end up deep in none of them.
The ones who go deep in the right tools get hired and promoted.

Which tools are you currently focused on?

♻️ Repost to help someone navigating the data tools landscape
💭 Tag a data analyst who needs to see this
📩 Get my full data analytics career guide: https://lnkd.in/gjUqmQ5H

-- Fight anxiety every time an uncertain job situation approaches

Author: sohansethi
Date: May 17, 2025
