URL: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7414675047442833408

The hottest hiring season (Jan - Mar) is here,

Here's a practical 90-day roadmap to ace your job search:
(With free resources below)

Did you spend 2025 sending hundreds of applications with zero results?
The issue isn't you. It's your system.

Here's a step-by-step roadmap to help you fix your job search system!

DAYS 1-7: AUDIT & STRATEGIZE
→ Review your last 50 applications: What got responses? 
→ Define 3-5 target roles and 20-30 companies 
→ Run your resume through Wisedoc AI Recruiting (https://www.wisedoc.net/) for ATS score.
→ Update LinkedIn with keywords from target job descriptions

Free tools:
Resume builder: (Wisedoc) - https://www.wisedoc.net/
Company research: Crunchbase - www.crunchbase.com
Salary data: (Levels) - levels.fyi

DAYS 8-30: OPTIMIZE & PRACTICE
→ Rewrite resume bullets using XYZ formula: "Accomplished [X] measured by [Y] by doing [Z]" and optimize your resume to get atleast a 90% ATS score
→ Prepare 5-7 STAR method interview stories 
→ Practice "tell me about yourself" in under 90 seconds 
→ Get free mock interviews at pramp.com

Free tools:
Interview prep: biginterview.com
LinkedIn headline: copy.ai
Networking templates: cultivatedculture.com

DAYS 31-90: EXECUTE & TRACK
→ Apply to 10-15 quality jobs daily (not 50 random ones) 
→ Send 20 personalized networking messages per week 
→ Follow up after 7 days on every message 
→ Track everything in a spreadsheet: company, role, date, status, etc. (This helps build accountability and consistency)

Free tools:
Job boards: builtin.com, indeed.com
Application tracker: notion.so/templates
Follow-up templates: themuse.com

--- Post 237 ---
Author: sohansethi
Date: May 17, 2025
Engagement: 255 reactions, 9 comments (Score: 1,365)
Media: image
URL: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7214407215166676992

99% of your happiness depends on this one life-lesson:

When you were in school,
You wanted to get into the best college.

When you sat for your placements,
You wanted to get the best salary in your batch.

When you were earning a good salary in India,
You wanted to escape and study abroad.

When you can study abroad (USA, UK, Canada, etc.),
You want to study at the most top-ranked university.

When you are in a foreign university,
You want to get scholarships and a high-paying part-time job.

When you are graduating from a university abroad,
You want to get a $100,000 offer.

When you are earning $100,000,
You want to earn $250,000.

When you are earning $250,000,
You want a big House, a sports car, and a ton of savings.

When you can have all of these,
You want to retire early.

Finally, when you have retired early,
You are still not satisfied and keep overthinking to want even more!

I don’t know if this stands true for you, however, this never-ending cycle of wants and desires is how I lived my life for the longest time.

Did this way of thinking bring me joy and happiness as it was supposed to do? Not at all.
It only made me feel miserable and doubt myself even after achieving all my dreams.

I soon realized it was important to let go and be grateful for how far I had come. Then once I looked back at my last 5 years and realized I had already achieved most of my dreams, it brought me so much peace and happiness.

So my message to you is this: There’s no need to look down on yourself by comparing your life to someone else’s successes. Your story might have a greater ending.

In retrospect, if you can achieve so much in just 5 years, my friend, you will achieve everything you ever wanted and much more in the next 50 years to come.

Until then, breathe a little, love yourself, and Take care ;)
Do you agree? Let me know in the comments below!

============================================================

--- Post 240 ---
Author: sohansethi
Date: May 17, 2025
Engagement: 215 reactions, 22 comments, 23 reposts (Score: 1,341)
Media: image
URL: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7284573469768368128

Here are 30 SQL questions that hiring managers love to ask:


A. WINDOWS FUNCTIONS:
1. Calculate the cumulative sum of sales for each region.
2. Rank products by their sales within each category.
3. Determine a running total of expenses by transaction date.
4. Identify the top 5 performing sales representatives by quarter.
5. Compute the year-over-year difference in revenue for each store.

B. COMMON TABLE EXPRESSIONS(CTE):
1. Write a recursive CTE to calculate Fibonacci numbers.
2. Use a CTE to identify the reporting hierarchy for a given employee.
3. Flatten a tree-structured data model using a CTE.
4. Use a recursive CTE to find the path between two nodes in a graph.
5. Analyze monthly sales trends using CTEs for better readability.

C. JOINS (INNER, OUTER, CROSS, SELF):
1. List customers who have purchased a specific product (Inner Join).
2. Identify orders that have no assigned delivery agent (Left Join).
3. Get products that were never sold (Right Join).
4. Generate all possible combinations of stores and their products (Cross Join).
5. Pair employees within the same team for mentorship opportunities (Self Join).

D. SUBQUERIES:
1. Find products priced above the average price in their category.
2. Identify employees who earn more than the average salary of their peers.
3. List customers who placed orders in every month of the year.
4. Retrieve the second and third highest salaries using subqueries.
5. Determine regions where no orders have been placed.

E. AGGREGATE FUNCTIONS:
1. Calculate the total sales for each product category.
2. Find the average number of orders per customer.
3. Identify the maximum, minimum, and average revenue generated by stores.
4. Count the number of active customers in each region.
5. Compute the variance and standard deviation of product prices.

F. INDEXING AND QUERY OPTIMIZATION:
1. Identify slow-running queries that could benefit from indexing.
2. Analyze the execution plan to optimize performance.
3. Compare query performance before and after adding an index.
4. Write a query to find unused indexes in a database.
5. Optimize a complex join query for faster execution.

Top 5 Platforms to Learn and Practice SQL:
1. Mode - https://lnkd.in/gFvXVFi2
2. DataLemur 🐵 (Ace the SQL & Data Interview) - https://datalemur.com
3. Dataford - https://www.dataford.io/
4. LeetCode - https://lnkd.in/gZMEsegU
5. StrataScratch - https://lnkd.in/gGtTvENK

--- Post 242 ---
Author: sohansethi
Date: Apr 17, 2026
Engagement: 165 reactions, 49 comments, 6 reposts (Score: 1,327)
Media: image
URL: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7448033764292616192

I spent 6 months building my O-1 Visa evidence,

Here's how my lawyer (Avalon L. Paul, Esq.) structured my case to meet the O-1 Criteria:

I walked into my O-1 consultation with:
-- A TEDx talk
-- Tons of research articles 
-- International media features
-- Judging prestigious competitions and hackathons

Avalon looked at everything and said: 
"You have strong evidence. But the way you've organized this won't work for USCIS."

I was shocked.
How could this get rejected?

Here’s what I didn't understand initially about the O-1:
I thought O-1 was about impressive accomplishments.
It's not. It's about proving those accomplishments meet specific USCIS criteria.

I am grateful I chose Avalon and Manifest Law as my legal counsel because:
-- She didn't just review my evidence, she researched how to position every piece strategically
-- She curated the case to my specific profile instead of using a generic template

Here's how my case was ultimately structured:

1. Media Features → Strategic Positioning

My version: "Featured on CNBC, Business Insider, Fox News"
Avalon's version: "Major national media sought beneficiary as recognized expert, reaching millions. Evidence of sustained acclaim."

They proved why the media wanted ME specifically.

2. Research articles → Quantified Impact

My version: List of 200+ article links
Avalon's version:
→ 50M+ aggregate views documented
→ Industry adoption metrics tracked
→ Evidence of field influence
→ Published in recognized media

They helped convey "Average articles" into "I shaped industry thinking."

3. Job Role → Critical Capacity

My version: "Unit Manager, Analytics"
Avalon's version:
→ Leadership role at Top healthcare company
→ LORs confirming critical/essential role
→ Quantified organizational impact

They showcased my role as critically essential to the industry.

Here's how they transformed my case:
A)They Found Evidence I Didn't Know Counted
B) They Built a Case that conveys industry-wide impact
C)The helped connect dots and linked my overall achievements together

A good lawyer doesn't just file your paperwork. 
They translate your achievements into the USCIS acceptable language.

A big shoutout to Avalon L. Paul, Esq. and Manifest Law for helping me draft my O-1 Petition.
They have a 97% O-1 approval rate and are providing free legal consultations to my network.
Free consultation link: https://lnkd.in/gQpAmBb7

This is proof that a good lawyer will completely transform your approval chances!

I don't want to gate-keep any information as I build my O-1 profile. I hope these series of posts are helpful for you.

♻️ Repost for someone building their O-1 case
💭 Comment "CFBR" to help others in your network.

--- Post 247 ---
Author: sohansethi
Date: May 17, 2025
Engagement: 232 reactions, 15 comments (Score: 1,310)
Media: image
URL: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7212107280035586048

“I am not good enough”
“I suck at my skills, everyone is better than me”
“After giving it my 100%, I am still not able to achieve it”

These were the words I said to myself after facing countless rejections during my job search process.

When I started this process, I was optimistic, grateful, and full of energy.
By the time I reached the end, I felt drained, sad, and partially depressed.

It was at that moment, I knew something needed to change.
 
Due to constant failures and rejections, I was stuck in a continuous loop of self-sabotage and negative self-talk. I knew this had to change before it was too late.

This is how I turned things around when everything was at its lowest:

1) Practice Mindfulness: There are many ways to practice mindfulness, including meditation, mindful eating/walking, etc. The crux is letting go of all the baggage and constantly being in the present.

2) Gratitude Journal: When things are at their lowest, your journal is your best friend. Pour down everything that comes to mind and write at least 5 things you are grateful for every single day.

3) Let your hard work make the noise: Instead of listening to the constant chatter of negative self-talk, go quiet internally and let your hard work make the noise today and I promise your success will follow tomorrow.

Following these techniques helped me get my life back on track and be optimistic again. 

When things finally turned back to normal and everything was going well, I took a look around and realized that almost everyone I know is going through or has gone through a similar phase of negative self-talk in the recent past.

This is what sparked me to share my journey on LinkedIn and help someone who might be in the same phase where I was just a year ago.

If you are feeling the above for whatever reason that is personal to you, just know that this feeling is temporary and everything will get better soon. The struggles you are going through have entered your life for a reason:
To make you a better version of yourself and help you grow. 

Lastly, if you feel like sharing your thoughts or seeking help, let me know in the comments below. 
I am here to support you all and I am sure you’ll overcome this phase soon!

Always remember the words of Buddha: 
You will win, not immediately, but DEFINITELY"

If you found this post relatable, then do like or share this post in your network. You might be helping it reach someone, who desperately needs to hear this.

That’s it guys, my second post in my series of “Mental Health for International Students”
If you enjoyed this, do keep an eye out for my last post in the series coming up next week.

Until next time, Take care and keep growing!

============================================================

Author: sohansethi

Here are 30 SQL questions that hiring managers love to ask:


A. WINDOWS FUNCTIONS:
1. Calculate the cumulative sum of sales for each region.
2. Rank products by their sales within each category.
3. Determine a running total of expenses by transaction date.
4. Identify the top 5 performing sales representatives by quarter.
5. Compute the year-over-year difference in revenue for each store.

B. COMMON TABLE EXPRESSIONS(CTE):
1. Write a recursive CTE to calculate Fibonacci numbers.
2. Use a CTE to identify the reporting hierarchy for a given employee.
3. Flatten a tree-structured data model using a CTE.
4. Use a recursive CTE to find the path between two nodes in a graph.
5. Analyze monthly sales trends using CTEs for better readability.

C. JOINS (INNER, OUTER, CROSS, SELF):
1. List customers who have purchased a specific product (Inner Join).
2. Identify orders that have no assigned delivery agent (Left Join).
3. Get products that were never sold (Right Join).
4. Generate all possible combinations of stores and their products (Cross Join).
5. Pair employees within the same team for mentorship opportunities (Self Join).

D. SUBQUERIES:
1. Find products priced above the average price in their category.
2. Identify employees who earn more than the average salary of their peers.
3. List customers who placed orders in every month of the year.
4. Retrieve the second and third highest salaries using subqueries.
5. Determine regions where no orders have been placed.

E. AGGREGATE FUNCTIONS:
1. Calculate the total sales for each product category.
2. Find the average number of orders per customer.
3. Identify the maximum, minimum, and average revenue generated by stores.
4. Count the number of active customers in each region.
5. Compute the variance and standard deviation of product prices.

F. INDEXING AND QUERY OPTIMIZATION:
1. Identify slow-running queries that could benefit from indexing.
2. Analyze the execution plan to optimize performance.
3. Compare query performance before and after adding an index.
4. Write a query to find unused indexes in a database.
5. Optimize a complex join query for faster execution.

The O-1A category is for people in the sciences, education, business or athletics. 

O1 Visa Criteria (You need at least 3 out of 8):

1. Awards & Recognition:
— Industry awards
— Hackathon wins
— Academic honors
Example: "Best startup in India 2019", "Best Web Design Dubai 2022", etc.

2. Membership in Exclusive Organizations:
— Professional associations requiring achievement
Example: GitHub Stars, IEEE Fellowship.

3. Published Articles/Research:
— Articles in esteemed journals
— Research papers
— Reputed industry articles 
Example: Technical articles with significant engagement by industry experts in your field.

4. Judging Others' Work:

— Patents

6. Recognized Articles About You:
— Media coverage

— News Articles 
Example: Featured in reputed tech publications or interviews.

7. High Salary/Remuneration:
— Salary in top 10% for your field/location 
Example: $180K+ for software engineers in most cities.

8. Critical Capacity
— Leadership positions at reputable companies 
Example: Tech lead at Fortune 500, Strong VC-backed Startup CTO.

Key Advantages of O1:

--- Post 254 ---
Author: sohansethi
Date: May 17, 2025
Engagement: 216 reactions, 19 comments (Score: 1,270)
Media: image
URL: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7267927909338636288

When you were in School,
You wanted to get into the best undergraduate college.

When you sat for your placements,
You wanted to get the best package in your batch.

When you were working in India,
You wanted to escape and further study abroad.

When you can study abroad (USA, UK, Canada, etc.),
You want to study at the most top-ranked university.

When you are in a foreign university,
You want to get scholarships and a high-paying part-time job.

When you are graduating and searching for jobs in a foreign country,
You want to get a $100,000 offer.

When you are earning $100,000,
You want to earn $250,000.

When you are earning $250,000,
You want a big House, a sports car, and a ton of savings.

When you can have all of these,
You want to retire early.

Finally, when you have retired early,
You are still not satisfied and keep torturing yourself to want even more!

I don’t know if this stands true for you. However, this never-ending cycle of wants and desires is how I have lived my life for the last few years.

However, only after returning to India and seeing my family after 3 years, 
I realized this probably wasn't the best approach.

Don’t get me wrong here, there is nothing bad with setting high-value goals and reaching your full potential; the worst part, however, is losing yourself completely while you are in the process of achieving them.

It is especially important in today’s world, where mental health is so disturbed and where comparisons are so unrealistic, that you choose to prioritize your wellbeing a little and pause for a moment, whenever required.

It is wiser to pause now than to one day realize that you crossed the line and things are now beyond repair, whether it be your health, mental well-being, or relationships.

We've got just this one life and we better make the most of each moment.

Do you agree?

============================================================

--- Post 256 ---
Author: sohansethi
Date: May 17, 2025
Engagement: 200 reactions, 26 comments (Score: 1,260)
Media: image
URL: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7221882812629499904

To the person who I owe everything in life: My Mom❤️ 

While growing up, she never forced me to pursue any set path in life. She simply led by example and gave me room to become whoever I wanted.

While growing up, I never realized what she must be going through.
 
Everyday, she was:
- Waking up at 5.30 AM
- Making sure I had my food ready for school/college
- Leaving home at 8 AM and traveling 1.5 hours to the office
- Working the entire day and coming back at 7 PM
- Cooking dinner, doing laundry and cleaning utensils
- Starting the same routine the next day once again

This is what I saw her doing almost every day without putting any burden of expectations on returning the hard work that she did for me.

It was only when I came to the US and didn't do even half of the things listed above that I realized what every mom goes through, still, I complained on most days about how tough my life was.

Yet, my mom somehow managed to do the above with a smile on her face!

The biggest lesson I learned from her was to be selfless in serving others without expecting anything in return, no matter their background or financial status.

I wish I am making her proud today by at least doing the small amount of work that I continue to do with my community here on LinkedIn.

Today being her birthday, I know she'll be reading this post, hence it's a special one. I hope this post continues to put a smile on her face. 

Happy Birthday Maa, thank you for everything!

--- Post 261 ---
Author: sohansethi
Date: May 17, 2025
Engagement: 222 reactions, 11 comments, 6 reposts (Score: 1,232)
Media: image
URL: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7254865932848672769

This is a famous interview question from Microsoft:

Question: Write a query to identify the top 2 Power Users who sent the highest number of messages on Microsoft Teams in August 2022. Display the IDs of these 2 users along with the total number of messages they sent. Output the results in descending order based on the count of the messages.

You can find full details here:
https://lnkd.in/gd7Jcp3w

Here’s my Step-by-Step Approach:

Step 1 - Understanding the table: 

The messages table contains columns like message_id, sender_id, receiver_id, content, and sent_date. We are particularly interested in sender_id (the user who sent the message) and sent_date (when the message was sent).

Step 2 - Filter by date: 

To focus on messages sent in August 2022, we need to filter the sent_date for this period (from '2022-08-01' to '2022-08-31').

Step 3 - Group by sender_id: 

For each user, we will count the number of messages sent in August 2022. Grouping by sender_id will allow us to aggregate messages by user.

Step 4 - Sort the result: 

We need to sort the users in descending order based on the number of messages they sent.

Step 5 - Limit to the top 2 users: 

After sorting, we will limit the result to the top 2 users.

This is a semi-medium level problem and should take about 5-10 minutes to get to the solution.

I hope this helps you ace your next SQL interview.

-- While everyone expects you to have it figured out, 
-- You might be questioning whether your degree was worth it

-- While classmates post success stories, 
-- You might be wondering why your journey looks different

I still remember the day after my graduation: 
1. My student visa clock started ticking 
2. My savings were slowly coming to an end
3. I had faced months in my job search without success
4. My confidence was dropping with each rejection email

I realized my degree was just the ticket to the game. The real test is what happens next!

Here's what Actually Worked For Me:
1. Stopped comparing my Chapter 1 to someone else's Chapter 20
2. Focused on small, achievable goals instead of landing the perfect job
3. Built skills through solid portfolio projects 
4. Connected with people 3-4 years ahead of me to learn from their experience

Today, looking back at my struggles: 
-- Those rejection emails built my resilience 
-- Those unanswered messages taught me persistence 
-- Those financial struggles shaped my work ethic 
-- Those moments of doubt made success even more sweeter

To every recent graduate feeling lost:
1. Your timeline is your own
2. Your struggles don't mean you're failing
3. Your journey isn't supposed to look like anyone else's

Remember: The same uncertainty that feels overwhelming today will be the story of strength you tell tomorrow.

Keep applying, keep learning, keep believing!

--- Post 264 ---
Author: sohansethi
Date: Apr 17, 2026

Media: image
URL: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7443295791060021248

I have conducted over 100 data analyst interviews.

Most candidates prepare for SQL syntax.
They memorize JOIN types. They practice window functions. They can write a CTE in their sleep.

Then I ask them a business problem and they freeze.

That is the gap. And it costs more candidates the offer than any technical mistake.

Here are the 7 SQL questions I ask in every interview and what I am actually testing for:

1. "We have a customer table and a transactions table. Write a query to find customers who have not made a purchase in the last 90 days."
Most candidates write the JOIN correctly.
What I am testing: Do you know to use LEFT JOIN and filter for NULL? Do you understand what "inactive customer" means to a business and why that number matters?

2. "Write a query to find the second highest salary in the employee table."
Simple on the surface.
What I am testing: Do you use LIMIT OFFSET, a subquery, or a window function? The approach tells me exactly where you are in your SQL journey.

3. "We have a sales table with daily revenue. Write a query to calculate a 7-day rolling average."
This is where most candidates slow down.
What I am testing: Window functions. Specifically AVG() OVER with a ROWS frame. If you cannot write this confidently you are not ready for a senior role.

4. "Find all duplicate records in this customer dataset and write a query to remove them while keeping the most recent entry."
Almost every candidate knows how to find duplicates.
What I am testing: Can you delete the right ones without destroying data? Do you use ROW_NUMBER() with a CTE? Data quality thinking matters more than the syntax here.

5. "We have a table of user events. Write a query to calculate the retention rate of users from week 1 to week 4."
This is a real business question disguised as a SQL problem.
What I am testing: Do you understand what retention means before you write a single line? Candidates who ask clarifying questions before coding always impress me more than ones who jump straight in.

6. "Our dashboard is running slowly. The query takes 45 seconds to load. What would you do?"
There is no single right answer here.
What I am testing: Do you think about indexes? Do you ask about table size? Do you consider query structure or missing WHERE clauses? Problem-solving approach matters more than the solution.

7. "Write a query to calculate month-over-month revenue growth for the last 12 months."
Every business tracks this. Every analyst should be able to build it.
What I am testing: LAG() function, date truncation, percentage change calculation. And - can you explain the output to a non-technical stakeholder?

Here is what candidates must do :

Practice the syntax. But understand why a business would ask that question.

That is what separates the candidates I call back from the ones I do not.

Save this. Come back to it before your next interview.

📩 Get my full SQL interview prep guide: https://lnkd.in/gjUqmQ5H

Top Certifications to Get a Job In Data Analytics:

(Based on 2026 job market)

The below roadmap has 8 tracks with 3 providers each. 
Choose your path depending on your skills and role.

Here is what the roadmap covers:

1. Data Analyst → Microsoft PL-900, PL-300 / Google Data Analytics Certificate / AWS Cloud Practitioner + Data Analytics Specialty

2. Business Intelligence Analyst → Microsoft PL-300, DP-500 / Google Business Intelligence Certificate / Tableau Desktop Specialist + Certified Data Analyst

3. Data Analytics Engineer → Microsoft DP-900, DP-203 / Google Professional Data Engineer / AWS Data Analytics Specialty + Databricks Data Engineer Associate

4. Data Visualization Specialist → Microsoft PL-300 / Tableau Desktop Specialist + Certified Data Analyst / Google Looker Business Intelligence Certification

5. Data Engineer (Analytics Focus) → Microsoft DP-203 / Google Professional Data Engineer / AWS Data Analytics Specialty + Databricks Data Engineer Professional

6. Machine Learning / Advanced Analytics → Microsoft AI-102, DP-100 / Google Professional Machine Learning Engineer / AWS Machine Learning Specialty

7. Data Governance & Data Quality → Microsoft DP-500 / Databricks Lakehouse Fundamentals / Collibra Certified Data Governance Professional

8. Big Data Analytics → Google Professional Data Engineer / AWS Data Analytics Specialty / Databricks Certified Data Engineer

Two things I want to be honest about:
If you have 3+ years of real work experience and a strong project portfolio - skip the beginner certifications. 

Hiring managers at good companies care more about what you have built than what you have passed.

If you are breaking in with no experience or switching careers - certifications are one of the fastest ways to close a credibility gap. 

The Google Data Analytics Certificate and PL-300 for Power BI are the ones I see on the most shortlisted resumes.

The mistake I see constantly: people collect certifications without knowing which role they are targeting. Pick a track first. Then get certified for that track.

I was one of them.

If you’re an international student, you know the challenges of the H1-B:
— Lottery Based: Selection is pure luck, not merit.
— Annual Cap: Only 85,000 new visas issued each year.
— High Rejection Odds: In 2024, more than 750,000 applicants applied for fewer than 1 in 7 spots.

However, here’s a good news I recently discovered:
There’s an alternative to the H-1B → the O-1 Visa

Unlike H1B, the O-1 visa has NO lottery, NO cap, and is more achievable than you think.
It also has a 95% approval rate!

Here are the advantages of the O-1 visa, but you need to speak to a lawyer to get specific legal advice:

1. No annual lottery cap - apply when you are ready.
2. 15-business day decision with premium processing.
3. Work for Multiple employers or start your own company.
4. 3-year approval with renewable extensions.
5. Excellent pathway to Green card via EB-1A/EB-2 NIW

The Best Part? You only need 3 out of 8 criteria. Example:
-- Publications/research papers
-- Leadership roles in organizations
-- Industry awards/recognition
-- Media coverage

Many high-achievers are already 80% qualified but don't realize it. 

An experienced immigration lawyer can help you build your case for the O-1 and can make a big difference in your O-1 approval rate.

I was once ghosted by a company after 7 rounds of interviews.
Here's how I turned this rejection into my biggest success:

Back in 2023, in the midst of my job search, I was interviewing for a major tech company. Everything was going well during the interview process (Or at least I thought it was!)

However one day, After 45 days of interviews (7 to be exact), presentations, and late-night prep sessions, the recruiter simply vanished. No email. No call. Nothing.

I was left devastated! 
-- I had spent countless hours preparing 
-- Missed out on other opportunities 
-- Was nearly exhausted with constant interviewing 
-- Almost gave up on my job search entirely

But this rejection became my greatest teacher!

I quickly learned from this failure and here’s what I did next: 
-- Used their feedback to upskill myself 
-- Networked genuinely instead of mass applying 
-- Targeted companies with transparent processes 
-- Built resilience through consistent action

My hardwork payed off and just 20 days later:
I landed a better role with 30% higher salary, an amazing culture and one of the top companies in the health insurance industry.

The lesson: Sometimes a rejection is simply the universe protecting you from the wrong environment and leading you to your greatest opportunity.

Every "No" is silently bringing you closer to your ONE "Yes"

Happy Independence Day, India ❤️ 
This is how I remember my home country:

It has been almost 3 years since I came to the USA and still haven't visited home.

-- While seeing the lights and glamour of Vegas is jaw-dropping,
-- Feeling the comfort of my house in India is a special feeling too.

-- While trying different cuisines from across the world is truly stimulating,
-- Having my favorite dish cooked by my mom is a special feeling too.

-- While celebrating professional milestones is a core memory,
-- Celebrating festivals back home is a special memory too.

-- While living my best life abroad is an exceptional experience,
-- Reliving the same silly moments with friends back home is a special feeling too.

I am immensely grateful to the USA for fulfilling all my dreams, 
However, I am grateful to India for giving me my Identity too.

This is a tribute to all those people leaving their homes to fulfill their dreams, I know the courage it takes the journey you go through.

I hope all your dreams come true soon!

♻ Share this post - If you are someone who left their home too.
💬 Comment your thoughts below - If this post resonated with you.

--- Post 278 ---
Author: sohansethi
Date: May 17, 2025
Engagement: 193 reactions, 14 comments, 1 reposts (Score: 1,107)
Media: image
URL: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7224793106754199552

Finding the right mentors at an early age made things very simple for me, Here's how you can find your mentors too!
(Check out my BONUS template below):

Sir Isaac Newton once said, “If I have seen further, it is by standing on the shoulders of giants.”

It honestly makes sense that having a solid group of people guiding you through life’s various obstacles makes things a lot easier indeed.

Here are the 6 types of Mentors that you can have in your life:

1) Hero Mentor:
This person is who you aspire to be, you want to be capable enough to do what he/she does daily. They are quite literally a Hero in your eyes indeed.

2) Peer Mentor:
This is the person who is at the same level as you. They could be a peer or a colleague you can learn from and share ideas candidly.

3) Reverse Mentor:
This person is your junior in some aspect. It could be someone just one level below you at work, school, etc. However, they solve problems differently and their unique perspective is precious too.

4) Optimism Giver:
This is the person who you approach when you want to feel worthy and confident. They can be anyone from your best friend to your parents. This person can help you overcome almost any obstacle in life.

5) Devil’s Advocate:
This person shows you your mirror when times are tough. They never sugar-coat things and say the truth as it is (and say it loud). Having a person who tells things without bullshit is the biggest asset one can have.

6) Connector:
This is a person who directs you to someone when you need them to help the most. They have a ton of social connections and always figure out a way around things. Having a connector is like having a cheat code for overcoming life’s million different challenges.

Alright, there we have it. This gives us an idea of whom to approach.
Now comes the question of how we approach our potential mentors.

It’s quite simple: By providing value and gradually building the relationship.

Here’s how I would do it:

“Hello Elon Musk,
I admire your work in the field of autonomous vehicles and would love to schedule a brief call to learn from you. I have been following your work for the past 6 months and I believe your recent posts on generative AI are truly groundbreaking. I believe my skills in Machine Learning could be beneficial to your current initiatives. Would you be available for a call next week?

Thank you for considering my request.
Best regards, 
[Your Name]”

Alright, I get it. It’s quite possible that Elon doesn't have time to reply to that message. But, maybe your next mentor might. Even if 2 out of 5 people respond to this message, I would say it's a win!
(And believe me, many people did reply to me using the template above)

Finally, Just take it slow initially. Learn about one another. Provide value and build the relationship over 3-4 weeks.

I’m sure the right mentor can change your life.
What are you waiting for? Go find your future mentor today!

Did you find this useful? Let me know in the comments below.

============================================================

BUT While big tech hiring is freezing, high-growth startups are ACTIVELY hiring right now.

Here are Top 15 AI Startups Actively Hiring TODAY:

1. Scale AI - scale.com/careers

2. Cohere - cohere.ai/careers

3. Jasper AI - jasper.ai/careers

4. Runway - runwayml.com/careers

6. Together AI - together.ai/careers

7. Harvey - harvey.ai/careers

8. Stability AI - stability.ai/careers

9. Stripe - stripe.com/careers

10. Figma - figma.com/careers

12. Notion - notion.so/careers

13. Canva - canva.com/careers

14. Discord - discord.com/careers

15. Epic Games - epicgames.com/careers

Why startups are BETTER than FAANG right now:
-- Faster interview processes (2-3 weeks vs 2-3 months)
-- Broader scope of responsibilities
-- Direct access to leadership
-- Equity holding potential

Here's My 3-Step Strategy to Get Hired at Startups:

1️⃣ Target Newly Funded Companies 
-- Check YC's latest batch 
-- Set up Crunchbase alerts for funding announcements 
-- Follow venture capital firms on Twitter

2️⃣ Skip HR, Go Directly to Decision-Makers 
-- Find CTOs/Engineering VPs on LinkedIn 
-- Cold Connect Template: "Hi [Name], I am highly interested in how [Company] is solving [specific problem]. My experience with [relevant skill] could help scale [specific feature]. I would love to connect for a quick 15 mins call" 
-- Be specific, not generic

3️⃣ Demonstrate Value BEFORE Applying 
-- Build something using their API 
-- Contribute to their open-source repos
-- Create a case study showing how you'd solve their problem 
-- Share this work when you reach out

REALITY CHECK: Big companies have 500+ applicants per role. Startups often have 20-50. Your odds just increased by 10x.

Always remember: Be useful first, then get hired.

Your dream job isn't at a company with a hiring freeze. It's at a startup desperate for talent like yours.

I hope this helps, All the best!

Author: sohansethi

URL: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7396923015252500480

Top 10 SQL Functions to ace your next analytics interview:

(Save this post now for your interview prep)

1. Basics
Retrieve, filter, and organize data efficiently using commands like SELECT, FROM, WHERE, ORDER BY, LIMIT, DISTINCT, and ALIAS.

2. Filtering & Conditions
Narrow down results precisely with logical operators such as AND, OR, NOT, BETWEEN, IN, and LIKE.

3. Aggregations
Summarize and analyze datasets using functions like COUNT, SUM, AVG, MIN, MAX, GROUP BY, and HAVING.

4. Joins
Combine data from multiple tables seamlessly through INNER JOIN, LEFT JOIN, RIGHT JOIN, FULL JOIN, and SELF JOIN.

5. Subqueries
Use nested SELECT statements within WHERE or FROM clauses to solve complex analytical problems efficiently.

6. Window Functions
Track trends, rank records, and analyze sequences using ROW_NUMBER, RANK, DENSE_RANK, LEAD, LAG, PARTITION BY, and ORDER BY.

7. Data Manipulation
Add, modify, or delete records in your database using INSERT INTO, UPDATE, and DELETE.

8. Data Definition
Define, modify, and manage your database structure with CREATE TABLE, ALTER TABLE, and DROP TABLE.

9. Advanced Analytics
Leverage powerful SQL tools like CTE, Views, CASE WHEN, COALESCE, and UNION / UNION ALL for cleaner, modular queries.

10. Performance Optimization
Improve query speed and overall efficiency through Indexing, Query Optimization, and EXPLAIN.

Top Platforms to Practice SQL:
-- DataLemur 🐵 (Ace the SQL & Data Interview) - https://datalemur.com/
-- StrataScratch - www.stratascratch.com/
-- LeetCode - https://leetcode.com/
-- HackerRank - https://lnkd.in/gDBqdrNP
-- Mode - https://mode.com/

Top 5 YouTubers to Learn Data Analytics:
1. Alex Freberg

Author: sohansethi
Date: Jun 21, 2025

Media: image
URL: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7331319094211727362

“I am not good enough, everyone is better than me”
These were the words I said to myself after facing countless rejections during my job search process.

When I started this process, I was optimistic, grateful, and full of energy.
By the time I reached the end, I felt drained, sad, and partially depressed.

It was at that moment, I knew something needed to change.

Due to constant failures and rejections, I was stuck in a continuous loop of self-sabotage and negative self-talk. I knew this had to change before it was too late.

This is how I turned things around when everything was at its lowest:

1. Practice Mindfulness: There are many ways to practice mindfulness, including meditation, mindful eating/walking, etc. The crux is letting go of all the baggage and constantly being in the present.

2. Gratitude Journal: When things are at their lowest, your journal is your best friend. Pour down everything that comes to mind and write at least 5 things you are grateful for every single day.

3. Let your hard work make the noise: Instead of listening to the constant chatter of negative self-talk, go quiet internally and let your hard work make the noise today and I promise your success will follow tomorrow.

Following these techniques helped me get my life back on track and be optimistic again.

If you are feeling the same, just know that this feeling is TEMPORARY and everything will get better soon!

The struggles you are going through have entered your life for a reason:
To make you a better version of yourself and help you grow.

If you found this post relatable, then do like and share this post in your network. You might be helping it reach someone, who desperately needs to hear this.

Lastly, Always remember the words of Buddha:
You will win, not immediately, but DEFINITELY"

Until next time, Take care and keep growing!

I applied to 500+ jobs....and got ZERO responses.

Here's my step-by-step guide to get your next interview using Cold-Emailing:

Step 1: Find the Right People 

→ Find hiring manager/recruiters for your target company on LinkedIn
→ Try Apollo.io for contact discovery 
→ Use Hunter.io for email verification

Step 2: Do Your Homework 

→ Research their recent LinkedIn activity 
→ Find common connections or interests
→ Identify their role in decision-making

→ Start with a genuine personalization 
→ Lead with value, not your needs 
→ Include one specific ask 
→ End with an easy a CTA

Best,
Sohan"

Tip 1: Keep it short, crisp and valuable for the reader

Author: sohansethi
Date: Feb 16, 2026
Engagement: 169 reactions, 16 comments, 9 reposts (Score: 1,023)
Media: image
URL: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7426636635527696384

Get hired in just 24 hours using CHATGPT,

Here are the top 5 prompts to ace your interview prep:

Interview preparation is a game of practice,
However, after submitting 50-100+ applications a day, there is hardly any time left for preparation.

I've compiled a list of the Top 5 ChatGPT Prompts that can actually get you hired with just 24 hours of interview prep:

1. Mock Interview Generator:
"Act as hiring manager for [role] with the below JD. Ask me behavioral and technical questions one at a time. After I answer, critique it: what was good, what to improve, how to make it more concise in STAR format."

2. Company Research:
"[Company] recently [product launch/challenge/news]. Analyze: what technical decisions they likely made, what problems they're solving now, what I'd ask their engineering team to show I understand their domain deeply."

3. Stakeholder Conflict Scenarios:
"Generate 3 difficult stakeholder scenarios for [role]: conflicting priorities, technical debt vs features, missed deadlines. For each, give me a framework to answer using: how I'd diagnose, communicate, and resolve. Include what NOT to say."

4. Technical Trede-off prep:
"For [specific technology/framework], explain 5 scenarios where I'd choose it vs alternatives. Include: performance implications, cost trade-offs, team expertise requirements. Make it interview-ready, not theoretical."

5. Weakness Reframer:
"My weakness is [actual weakness]. Turn this into an honest answer that shows I'm working on it with specific steps I'm taking. Create and answer in STAR Format."

Copy and paste the above prompts into ChatGPT along with your resume and job description and see the magic happen!

Why this works:
Generic prep = generic answers = no offer 
Specific prep = authentic answers = you get hired

Author: sohansethi
Date: Mar 18, 2026
Engagement: 184 reactions, 4 comments, 23 reposts (Score: 1,006)
Media: image
URL: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7433513479418060800

I am hiring you as a Data Analyst in 2026:

(If you can answer these 30 SQL questions)

A. WINDOWS FUNCTIONS:
1. Calculate the cumulative sum of sales for each region.
2. Rank products by their sales within each category.
3. Determine a running total of expenses by transaction date.
4. Identify the top 5 performing sales representatives by quarter.
5. Compute the year-over-year difference in revenue for each store.

B. COMMON TABLE EXPRESSIONS(CTE):
1. Write a recursive CTE to calculate Fibonacci numbers.
2. Use a CTE to identify the reporting hierarchy for a given employee.
3. Flatten a tree-structured data model using a CTE.
4. Use a recursive CTE to find the path between two nodes in a graph.
5. Analyze monthly sales trends using CTEs for better readability.

C. JOINS (INNER, OUTER, CROSS, SELF):
1. List customers who have purchased a specific product (Inner Join).
2. Identify orders that have no assigned delivery agent (Left Join).
3. Get products that were never sold (Right Join).
4. Generate all possible combinations of stores and their products (Cross Join).
5. Pair employees within the same team for mentorship opportunities (Self Join).

D. SUBQUERIES:
1. Find products priced above the average price in their category.
2. Identify employees who earn more than the average salary of their peers.
3. List customers who placed orders in every month of the year.
4. Retrieve the second and third highest salaries using subqueries.
5. Determine regions where no orders have been placed.

E. AGGREGATE FUNCTIONS:
1. Calculate the total sales for each product category.
2. Find the average number of orders per customer.
3. Identify the maximum, minimum, and average revenue generated by stores.
4. Count the number of active customers in each region.
5. Compute the variance and standard deviation of product prices.

F. INDEXING AND QUERY OPTIMIZATION:
1. Identify slow-running queries that could benefit from indexing.
2. Analyze the execution plan to optimize performance.
3. Compare query performance before and after adding an index.
4. Write a query to find unused indexes in a database.
5. Optimize a complex join query for faster execution.

These are the 30 most commonly asked SQL questions in Technical Interviews.
So begin by practicing them now!

--- Post 300 ---
Author: sohansethi
Date: Apr 17, 2026
Engagement: 114 reactions, 41 comments, 10 reposts (Score: 1,000)
Media: image
URL: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7445162496891666432

I spoke to 20+ students who landed internships and full time roles this year.

Here is what every single one of them had in common.

-- 𝗧𝗵𝗲𝘆 𝗮𝗽𝗽𝗹𝗶𝗲𝗱, 𝗮 𝗹𝗼𝘁

The average across all 20 was 1000+ applications before landing an offer. Nobody got lucky on application 12. The ones who got discouraged at 50 are still searching.

Volume matters. But volume without strategy is just noise.

-- 𝗧𝗵𝗲𝘆 𝗮𝗽𝗽𝗹𝗶𝗲𝗱 𝘄𝗶𝘁𝗵𝗶𝗻 𝟮𝟰 𝗵𝗼𝘂𝗿𝘀 𝗼𝗳 𝗽𝗼𝘀𝘁𝗶𝗻𝗴

Every single person I spoke to mentioned this unprompted.
Jobs posted more than 24 hours ago already have hundreds of applications. The ones posted in the last few hours have almost none.

They checked these 5 platforms daily for fresh postings:
-- LinkedIn Jobs
-- Indeed
-- Handshake (best for students and new grads)
-- Wellfound (best for startup roles)
-- Levels.fyi (best for tech and compensation data)

Set job alerts. Check them every morning. Apply the same day.

-- 𝗧𝗵𝗲𝘆 𝘀𝘁𝗮𝗿𝘁𝗲𝗱 𝘄𝗶𝘁𝗵 𝘀𝘁𝗮𝗿𝘁𝘂𝗽𝘀, 𝗻𝗼𝘁 𝗯𝗶𝗴 𝘁𝗲𝗰𝗵

This surprised me the most.

Most of them did not start by targeting Google or Amazon. They targeted Series A and Series B startups first.

Why? Less competition. Faster hiring process. More responsibility from day one. And the experience looked strong on a resume when they eventually applied to larger companies.

Start where you can get in. Use that experience to get where you want to go.

-- 𝗧𝗵𝗲𝘆 𝘂𝘀𝗲𝗱 𝗔𝗜 𝘁𝗼 𝗳𝗶𝗻𝗲 𝘁𝘂𝗻𝗲 𝗲𝘃𝗲𝗿𝘆 𝗿𝗲𝘀𝘂𝗺𝗲

Not to write their resume from scratch. To pressure test it.

They pasted the job description and their resume into Claude and asked: "What is missing? What keywords am I not matching? What would a recruiter flag as weak?"
Then they fixed it. Every single application.

Generic resumes get generic results.

-- 𝗧𝗵𝗲𝘆 𝗳𝗼𝗹𝗹𝗼𝘄𝗲𝗱 𝗼𝗻𝗲 𝗿𝗲𝘀𝘂𝗺𝗲 𝘁𝗲𝗺𝗽𝗹𝗮𝘁𝗲 𝗮𝗻𝗱 𝘀𝘁𝘂𝗰𝗸 𝘁𝗼 𝗶𝘁

No fancy designs. No colors. No graphics. No photos.

Clean. One page. ATS friendly. Achievement-focused bullets in the format: action verb + task + measurable result.

Here is the exact resume template that got interviews at Google, Microsoft, and more:
https://lnkd.in/gPFK4eEy 

Save that link. Use that template. Stop overthinking the design.

The students who landed roles this year did not have better backgrounds than the ones still searching.

They had a better system.

-- 𝗧𝗵𝗲𝘆 𝗮𝗽𝗽𝗹𝗶𝗲𝗱 𝘄𝗶𝘁𝗵𝗶𝗻 𝟮𝟰 𝗵𝗼𝘂𝗿𝘀 𝗼𝗳 𝗽𝗼𝘀𝘁𝗶𝗻𝗴

Every single person I spoke to mentioned this unprompted.
Jobs posted more than 24 hours ago already have hundreds of applications. The ones posted in the last few hours have almost none.

They checked these 5 platforms daily for fresh postings:
-- LinkedIn Jobs
-- Indeed
-- Handshake (best for students and new grads)
-- Wellfound (best for startup roles)
-- Levels.fyi (best for tech and compensation data)

Set job alerts. Check them every morning. Apply the same day.

-- 𝗧𝗵𝗲𝘆 𝘀𝘁𝗮𝗿𝘁𝗲𝗱 𝘄𝗶𝘁𝗵 𝘀𝘁𝗮𝗿𝘁𝘂𝗽𝘀, 𝗻𝗼𝘁 𝗯𝗶𝗴 𝘁𝗲𝗰𝗵

This surprised me the most.

Most of them did not start by targeting Google or Amazon. They targeted Series A and Series B startups first.

Why? Less competition. Faster hiring process. More responsibility from day one. And the experience looked strong on a resume when they eventually applied to larger companies.

Start where you can get in. Use that experience to get where you want to go.

-- 𝗧𝗵𝗲𝘆 𝘂𝘀𝗲𝗱 𝗔𝗜 𝘁𝗼 𝗳𝗶𝗻𝗲 𝘁𝘂𝗻𝗲 𝗲𝘃𝗲𝗿𝘆 𝗿𝗲𝘀𝘂𝗺𝗲

Not to write their resume from scratch. To pressure test it.

They pasted the job description and their resume into Claude and asked: "What is missing? What keywords am I not matching? What would a recruiter flag as weak?"
Then they fixed it. Every single application.

Generic resumes get generic results.

-- 𝗧𝗵𝗲𝘆 𝗳𝗼𝗹𝗹𝗼𝘄𝗲𝗱 𝗼𝗻𝗲 𝗿𝗲𝘀𝘂𝗺𝗲 𝘁𝗲𝗺𝗽𝗹𝗮𝘁𝗲 𝗮𝗻𝗱 𝘀𝘁𝘂𝗰𝗸 𝘁𝗼 𝗶𝘁

No fancy designs. No colors. No graphics. No photos.

Clean. One page. ATS friendly. Achievement-focused bullets in the format: action verb + task + measurable result.

Here is the exact resume template that got interviews at Google, Microsoft, and more:
https://lnkd.in/gPFK4eEy 

Save that link. Use that template. Stop overthinking the design.

The students who landed roles this year did not have better backgrounds than the ones still searching.

They had a better system.

Which of these are you not doing yet?

90% of us will FAIL In Our Job Search!

Here's 4 reasons why most people will struggle in 2025:

1. Ignoring ATS Screening:
Companies use AI and ATS systems to filter resumes. Job seekers who don't understand keyword optimization get eliminated before their applications reach the human eyes.

2. Weak Online Presence:
No LinkedIn optimization, outdated profiles, or zero industry engagement. In 2025, your digital brand is your biggest impression.

3. Skipping the research:
Candidates spend 5 minutes applying to a job, but 0 seconds understanding whether the job matches their skill sets. Such candidates get rejected instantly.

4. Poor Networking Strategy: 
Still relying solely on job boards instead of building genuine professional relationships. Almost 80% of positions are filled through networking today.

Using the same strategies will often lead to the same response, i.e. 0 callbacks!

Here's what actually works:

A. Build Relationships First:
Engage with industry content, comment thoughtfully on posts, and reach out for informational interviews. Focus on giving value before asking for opportunities.

B. Focus on value-oriented applications:
Spend 10-15 minutes per application learning about the company, their challenges, recent wins. Mention something specific in your cover letter and apply with ATS tailored applications.

Sometimes the smallest shifts make the biggest difference.

The job market is tough, but it's not impossible. 
Stop playing the numbers game. Start playing the value game.

Happy World Mental Health Day❤️
If you’ve ever suffered from Anxiety, Depression, Imposter Syndrome (or know someone who might), then please keep reading:

It was almost 2 years ago that I started facing acute anxiety due to my job search process. I wish I had a better way of dealing with things, but unfortunately, I did not. 

The result was extreme deterioration of my mental and physical health.
Due to this, mental health is a topic that I deeply resonate with. 

To make sure not a single person has to go through the same experience, here is a list of resources that I have found extremely useful in the past 2 years:

1. Top 7 Creators to Follow:
a. Linda Le🌻
b. Alyssa Petersel, LMSW
c. Melissa Doman, M.A.
d. Ankur Warikoo
e. Lisa Orbé-Austin, PhD
f. Karishma Mehta
g. Natasha Bowman, JD, SPHR

2. Self-Care and Wellness Apps:
a. Headspace - www.headspace.com
b. Calm - www.calm.com
c. Waking Up - www.wakingup.com

3. Books helped me along the way:
a. When the Body says no - Gabor Maté
b. The Mindful Way Through Depression - Jon Kabat-Zinn

4. Online Therapy and Counseling:
a. BetterHelp - www.betterhelp.com
b. Talkspace - www.talkspace.com

5. The following strategies helped me immensely on a day-to-day basis:

a. Identify the stressors: The first step is to identify where the stress is stemming from. Whether it is your work, friends, or family, note everything down.

b. Create boundaries: It is essential to set boundaries. Setting a hard start and stop time daily and stepping away from your stressors is crucial for your mental health.

c. Refill your cup: Even though you cannot take a vacation in the middle of your day, it is essential to do something that you love for at least 15 minutes a day. This will help reduce stress and bring back that vital energy you need and deserve.

I assure you that following these steps daily will reduce anxiety and will slowly fill you with life over time.

6. Contact the below helpline if you need immediate support:
a. 7 Cups - www.7cups.com
b. Befrienders Worldwide - https://befrienders.org/

Please don’t take this as an informative post, take this as a request to SLOW DOWN.

It is not worth it to damage your mental health, no matter what the cost is.
Let us learn to breathe a little, love ourselves, and Take care!

♻ Share this post, maybe someone needs to read this desperately!

Author: sohansethi

There are 2 types of data analysts emerging inside companies right now.

Those who fear AI.
And those who use it as leverage.

The gap between them is growing faster than expected.

Here is what is actually happening:

The analyst who fears AI:
Strong fundamentals. Knows SQL well. Builds clean dashboards. Never misses a deadline.

But every time AI comes up in a meeting, he goes quiet.

Recently, he spent 4 days building a report that a colleague completed in just 6 hours using AI tools. Same quality. Same accuracy.

When asked why he didn’t use the tools available, his answer was simple:
“I just feel more comfortable doing it my way.”

That mindset is understandable.
But it comes with a hidden cost.

His output per week has stayed flat for the last 2 years.
Meanwhile, his colleague’s output has doubled in just 6 months.

When promotion conversations happen—and they will—it becomes difficult to advocate for someone whose productivity hasn’t evolved while the role itself has changed.

The analyst who uses AI as leverage:
Less experience on paper. Newer to the tools. Asked more questions in her first month than anyone else on the team.

But from day one, she treated AI like a competitive advantage.

She uses tools like Claude to pressure test her analysis before presenting. She drafts executive summaries with AI and refines them. She even built a prompt library for the team—saving over 10 hours every week.

Last month, she caught an error in a vendor dataset that could have caused a significant financial loss.

She found it because she had time to look.
She had time because AI handled the parts of her job that used to consume her entire day.

Here is the reality every analyst needs to understand:

AI is not replacing your job.
But an analyst who uses AI is already doing more than you in less time.

That is who you are competing against for the next promotion.
That is who you are competing against for the next role.

The analysts who will still be relevant in 3 years are not the ones who know the most SQL.

They are the ones who know how to combine their domain expertise with AI to produce output that neither could produce alone.

That combination is the skill.

Which type of analyst are you right now?

--- Post 308 ---
Author: sohansethi

URL: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7441230097170468864

CV vs Resume confusion.

This is one of the most common mistakes I see from international candidates applying to US jobs.

As a Data Analytics Manager who has reviewed hundreds of applications - I can tell within seconds which candidates understand the US job market and which ones do not.

Sending the wrong document signals that you do not.

Here is the difference:

CV (Curriculum Vitae)
-- Used in academic, research, and international settings
-- Includes everything - publications, awards, research, certifications
-- Length does not matter. 3 pages, 10 pages. All acceptable.
-- Common in Europe, Asia, and the Middle East

Resume
-- Used in the US and Canada for corporate jobs
-- Concise snapshot of your most relevant experience for one specific role
-- 1 page under 10 years of experience. 2 pages maximum after that.
-- Every line should earn its place. If it does not add value, cut it.

The mistake I see constantly:
International candidates move to the US, copy their CV into a Word document, rename it "Resume.docx" and start applying.

The document is still 4 pages.
It still has high school achievements.
It still has a photo in the corner.

A US recruiter opens it and moves on in 15 seconds.

Not because the candidate is unqualified.
Because the document signals they have not done the work to understand the market they are entering.

Here is what a strong US resume does differently:
-- Leads with a headline that tells the recruiter who you are in one line
-- Bullet points in the format: action verb + task + measurable result
-- Only includes experience relevant to the role you are applying for
-- Keyword optimized for ATS before a human ever sees it
-- No photo, no date of birth, no marital status, no references

One more thing most people miss:
Your resume should not be the same document for every application.

The job description tells you exactly what the company wants.
Your resume should reflect that language back to them.

A candidate who tailors their resume to each role will always outperform one who sends the same document 200 times.

Know which document to send.
Know how to build it correctly.

That is the baseline before anything else in your job search matters.

Are you still sending a CV to US employers?

♻️ Repost - every international candidate in your network needs to see this
💭 Tag someone who just started their US job search
📩 Get my full resume guide for international candidates: https://lnkd.in/gjUqmQ5H

Author: sohansethi

This Resume Cheat-sheet has already helped 10,000+ Job Seekers:


Your resume is your entry ticket to a job interview,
And with the right resume, you can open some great interview opportunities for yourself.

Here’s exactly how I would structure a good resume:

1. Education:
-- Let the hiring manager know the knowledge that you’ve been trained for.

2. Skills:
-- Convey the skills that you can contribute to the team.

3. Work Experience:
-- Showcase how your previous experience will help you successfully carry out the responsibilities of the role that you’re interviewing for.

4. Projects:
This section helps you portray your expertise in a broad range of skill sets.

5. Leadership and Awards:
Portraying a holistic personality further adds to the credibility of you as a candidate.

Having your resume structured this way will be the easiest way for the hiring manager to select you as a potential candidate and send out an interview invite.

In addition, here are a few tips for a fully optimized resume:

-- If you have less than 10 years of experience, always keep a 1-page resume.
-- Make sure to include tons of metrics and data to support your achievements
-- Always format your bullet like this: Accomplished [X] as measured by [Y], by doing [Z].
-- Tailor a unique resume for each job application to make it ATS-friendly.

AI Tools To Craft The Perfect Resume:
1. Rezi - https://www.rezi.ai/
2. Zety - https://zety.com/
3. Kickresume - https://lnkd.in/gqUyT4Bw
4. Novorésumé - https://novoresume.com/
5. Teal - https://www.tealhq.com/
6. resume.io - https://resume.io/

If you are currently struggling to get callbacks, try these suggestions.
I assure you, that by implementing them for just 2-3 weeks, you’ll see an increase in the number of callbacks that you get.

My aim with this post is to help everyone currently in the job market to excel in their job search process.

Feel free to use and take inspiration from my cheat-sheet below.
I hope you get an interview call soon, All the best!

--- Post 320 ---
Author: sohansethi
Date: Apr 17, 2026
Engagement: 85 reactions, 45 comments, 7 reposts (Score: 889)
Media: image
URL: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7450972314973921281

An Ex-USCIS officer's feedback saved my O-1 petition:

My O-1 case was recently reviewed by an ex-USCIS officer with the help of Manifest Law, Evan J. Law has spent years adjudicating O-1 petitions.

Evan reviewed my case the way USCIS would on decision day.

Here's what Evan found:
My O-1 case evidence was filled with gaps.

1. My evidence lacked context:
I listed achievements without showing how they compare to others in my field.

2. My recommendation letters were generic:
They praised me but didn't prove specific impact or unique contributions.

3. My critical role wasn't documented properly:
I showed I was good at my job, not that I was essential to the organization.

These weren't fatal flaws. But they were gaps USCIS would question.

Evan asked questions I never considered:
"Why is this extraordinary vs. just good?"
"What proves this is national-level recognition?"
"If I'm skeptical, what's your strongest proof?"

These are the actual questions USCIS officers ask when reviewing cases.

Here’s how I improved my case after the review:
Before: I was hoping my evidence was strong enough
After: I knew exactly what USCIS needed to see and provided proof of impact.

I added:
→ Comparative benchmarks proving "top of field"
→ Specific examples and metrics to all letters
→ Documentation of organizational dependence on my work

Why This Mattered:
Most lawyers know how to file O-1 petitions.
Very few know how USCIS officers actually evaluate them.

Manifest Law's ex-USCIS officer review showed me the difference between "strong evidence" and "approval-ready evidence."

BONUS: They are offering free case evaluation for my network.
Book here: https://lnkd.in/gQpAmBb7

You might have everything you need.
But how you present it to the USCIS can change everything.

"I don't have $100,000 for the new H1-B Filing fees. 

That means, I'll LEAVE the country in the next 3 months"

This is what my friend told me yesterday.
And I am sure the same thought would be running through your own mind too.

However, I told my friend there is an alternative.
And I'll share the same with you too:
There's an alternative to the H-1B → the O-1 Visa

Unlike H1B, the O1 visa has NO lottery, NO $100K fees, and is more achievable than you think!
It also has a 95% approval rate!
Here's what makes the O-1 visa special, but you need to speak to a lawyer to get specific legal advice:

Key Advantages of O-1:
-- No annual lottery cap - apply when you are ready.
-- 15-business day decision with premium processing.
-- Work for multiple employers or start your own company.
-- 3-year approval with renewable extensions.
-- Significantly lower filing costs.
-- Excellent pathway to Green card via EB-1A/EB-2 NIW

The Best Part? 
You might be already 80% qualified for the O-1 visa but don't realize it.

You only need 3 out of these 8 criteria:
1. Awards & Recognition:
-- Industry awards, Hackathon wins, Academic honors

2. Membership in Exclusive Organizations: 
-- Professional associations requiring achievement

3. Published Articles/Research: 
-- Articles in esteemed journals, Research papers

4. Judging Others' Work: 
-- Judging professional competitions, Peer reviews

5. Original Contributions: 
-- Patents, High impact open-source projects

6. Recognized Articles About You: 
-- Media coverage, Press Interviews, News Articles

7. High Salary/Remuneration: 
-- Salary in top 10% for your field/location

8. Critical Capacity: 
-- Leadership positions at reputable companies

BONUS: Get experienced help for your O-1 visa, with trusted lawyers by Manifest Law
They even offer a get your Visa Approved or Money Back guarantee (for certain plans; conditions apply).

𝐁𝐞𝐠𝐢𝐧𝐧𝐞𝐫 𝐋𝐞𝐯𝐞𝐥 (𝐖𝐞𝐞𝐤 𝟏-𝟐):
— Basics of SELECT, WHERE, GROUP BY
— INNER, LEFT, RIGHT Joins
— Aggregation Functions (SUM, COUNT, AVG, etc)

𝐈𝐧𝐭𝐞𝐫𝐦𝐞𝐝𝐢𝐚𝐭𝐞 𝐋𝐞𝐯𝐞𝐥 (𝐖𝐞𝐞𝐤 𝟑-𝟒):
— Window Functions (ROW_NUMBER, RANK)
— CTEs and Subqueries
— CASE statements for conditional logic

𝐀𝐝𝐯𝐚𝐧𝐜𝐞𝐝 𝐋𝐞𝐯𝐞𝐥 (𝐖𝐞𝐞𝐤 𝟓-𝟔):

— Data Modeling
— Complex Joins & Relationships

5 Simple Steps to Land Your Dream Job Using LinkedIn:
(This strategy got me multiple FAANG Interviews)

Building my LinkedIn profile was the best decision of my life.

After building my LinkedIn presence to 100,000+ followers and helping 1000’s of students, I've seen what actually works for job seekers.

Here's my straightforward 5-step system to landing your next job:

Step 1: Optimize Your Profile Like a Landing Page
What to do: Treat your LinkedIn profile as your personal website.

How to do it:
-- Write a headline that shows your value, not just your title: "Sales Manager who increased revenue by 40%" vs "Sales Manager"
-- Add a professional headshot (profiles with photos get 21x more views)
-- Include 3-5 relevant skills in your summary with specific examples
-- Turn on "Open to Work" so recruiters can see it

Time needed: 2 hours, one-time setup

Step 2: Network and Engage with Potential Employers
What to do: Become visible to the companies you want to work for.

How to do it:
-- Follow employees from 10-15 target companies
-- Connect with 30-50 employees each week with personalized messages
-- Find my Networking template here: https://bit.ly/3ZTs5Ob
-- Follow-up and ask for referrals to boost your job applications

Time needed: 15 minutes daily

Step 3: Create Content That Shows Your Expertise
What to do: Post valuable content to demonstrate your knowledge.

How to do it:
-- Share one industry insight or lesson learned each week
-- Use this format: Problem + Your Solution + Result/Takeaway
-- Post on Tuesday-Thursday between 8-10 AM for maximum reach

Time needed: 30 minutes weekly

Step 4: The Strategic Outreach Method
What to do: Connect with hiring managers and recruiters the right way.

How to do it:
-- After applying for a job, find the hiring manager on LinkedIn
-- Send a connection request: "Hi [Name], I just applied for the [Role] position and would love to connect. I'm particularly excited about [specific company initiative]."
-- Wait 2-3 days after they accept, then send a follow-up message
-- Share why you're genuinely interested in their company (mention recent news, values, or projects)

Time needed: 10 minutes per application

Step 5: Track Your Progress and Double Down
What to do: Monitor what's working and do more of it.

How to do it:
-- Check LinkedIn analytics weekly (who's viewing your profile?)
-- Note which posts get the most engagement from your target audience
-- Keep a simple spreadsheet: Company, Contact, Date Reached Out, Response
-- Send a weekly update to your network about your job search progress

Time needed: 15 minutes weekly

BONUS: Optimize your LinkedIn profile with Wisedoc’s AI Tool
Link: https://bit.ly/4jysnBj

Remember: Consistency beats perfection.

Start with Step 1 today, add one step slowly and steadily.
I assure you, the results will soon follow.

I hope this helps, All the best in your job search!

P.S: I post FREE job search tips and resources. Connect with me for more such resources daily.

1 month ago, a 60 year old Wells Fargo employee died at her desk,
1 week ago, a 26 year old EY employee died due to work-related stress,
Who’s next? Anybody we know can be one of these tragic cases!

I’m sure you’ve all been there:

-- Late nights
-- Early mornings
-- Endless meetings
-- Skipping lunches
-- Neglecting health and family
-- Prioritizing work over everything!

However, even after all this, do you think all this stress was worth it?

How long before it’s too late?
How long before the damage is done?

Nothing matters more than one’s health,
Nothing matters more than one’s LIFE.

If you feel burnt out, now is the best time to take action.

The following strategies helped me immensely during my burnout phase.
If you feel burnt out, please follow the tips below:

-- Identify the stressors: The first step is to identify where the stress is stemming from. Whether it is your work, friends, or family, note everything down.

-- Create boundaries: It is essential to set boundaries. Setting a hard start and stop time daily and stepping away from your stressors is crucial for your mental health.

-- Refill your cup: Even though you cannot take a vacation in the middle of your job, it is essential to do something that you love for at least 15 minutes a day. This will help reduce stress and bring back that vital energy you need and deserve.

I assure you that following these steps daily will reduce anxiety and will slowly fill you with life over time.

Also, contact the below helpline's if you need immediate support:
1. 7 Cups - www.7cups.com
2. Befrienders Worldwide - https://befrienders.org/

Please don’t take this as an informative post, take this as a request to SLOW DOWN.

It is not worth it to damage your mental health, no matter what the cost is.
Let us learn to breathe a little, love ourselves, and Take care!

Most of you reading this post (just like me) would be lucky enough to maintain a healthy work-life balance, however someone very close to us might not.

You never know when someone in your friends or family might become a tragic news article.

♻ Share this post, maybe someone needs to desperately read this!
💬 If you found this post relatable, comment your thoughts below 👇

Here's Every Tool You Need to Land Your Next Role:

Job hunting today is not just about sending resumes, it’s about using the right tools at every stage of your journey.

Here’s a breakdown of how modern job seekers can navigate the process effectively : 

- Skill Building
Platforms like Coursera, Udemy, and edX help you upskill.
Assess your progress with Codility or TestGorilla, and stay active in communities like Reddit, Inc. and Discord.

- Resume & Profile Optimization
Build standout resumes with Kickresume, Rezi, or FlowCV.
Use Teal, ResumAI by Wonsulting, or VMock to analyze and optimize your resume.
Create portfolios on Framer, Notion, or Behance and polish your LinkedIn with tools like Taplio and Crystal.

- Job Discovery Platforms
Find your dream job using LinkedIn, Indeed, or Glassdoor.
For remote roles, explore We Work Remotely, Remotive, and JustRemote.
AI job matchers like Simplify and TopAI personalize your search.

- Networking & Personal Branding
Grow your presence using Taplio, LinkHelp, or Shield.
Network on Lunchclub, Shapr, or AngelList, and attend events via HOPIN or Eventbrite.
Showcase your personal brand with sites built on Framer, Super, or Notion.

- Interview Preparation
Practice mock interviews on Pramp, interviewing.io, and MockMate.
Get coached by AI tools like Yoodli and PrepAI, and refine your answers with InterviewBit or LeetCode.
Record and review your performance with Huru or SparkHire.

- Application & Tracking
Keep track of job applications using Huntr or Careerflow.ai.
Automate submissions with Loopcv or ApplyAI, and generate AI-written cover letters using Kickresume AI or Jasper.
Track progress with tools like ClickUp or Airtable.

From learning new skills to landing offers - this ecosystem covers it all.

Today I have all my success, because I was always surrounded by powerful women💜

Growing up, I never fully understood the sacrifices my mom made. She never pushed me in any direction—she simply led by example, showing me the power of resilience, hard work, and unconditional love.

Every single day, she:
🌸 Woke up at 5:30 AM to prepare meals for me
🌸 Traveled 1.5 hours to work after making sure I had what I needed
🌸 Spent the entire day working, only to return home and continue working—cooking, cleaning, and taking care of us
🌸 Repeated the same exhausting cycle without ever complaining

And yet, she always smiled.

It was only when I moved to the U.S. and struggled to handle even half of her responsibilities that I realized what every mother goes through.

Indian culture has always symbolized women as Devi's(Goddesses), and today I can totally relate why!

Today I can proudly say, I owe all my success to my mom, my friends, my colleague's and all the powerful women out there.

On this Women’s Day, I celebrate her—and every woman who:
💜 Lifts others up without asking for anything in return
💜 Balances career, family, and personal dreams with grace
💜 Pushes forward despite unseen struggles

The biggest lesson my mom taught me? True strength is in selfless service.

Here's a few women creators from whom I've learnt a lot and they have had the most impact in my own journey:

Bhavya Passi, Toni Xu, Ariana D., Meri Nova, Niharikaa Kaur Sodhi, Shreya Mishra Reddy, Melissa Grabiner, Roberta Storey, Shreya Mehta 🚀, Ritika Saraswat, Soundarya (SB) Balasubramani, Anna Chernyshova, Sravya Madipalli, Swagata Ashwani, Supreet Kaur, Sravya Madipalli, Dawn Choo, Gina Acosta Gutiérrez, Jess Ramos, MSBA

I put everything on the line to come to USA:

Quit my stable job. Took $50,000 in debt. Left everyone I loved behind.

Everyone said I am making a huge mistake.

I gave up my: 
→ Comfortable salary 
→ Comfort of Family 
→ Financial security 
→ Peace of mind

What I got instead:
→ Pure survival mode 
→ Cleaning dishes at $13/hour 
→ Sleeping 3-4 hours daily 
→ Applying to 200+ jobs weekly 
→ Eating $2 meals to save money 
→ Missing every celebration back home

My lowest point was having $50,000 in debt and 0 Job offers after 3,000+ applications.

I sat in my university library one day and questioned everything.
"Did I destroy my life for nothing?"

However, at this exact moment, something magical happened:
-- I Stopped waiting for perfect opportunities. 
-- I Started creating them relentlessly

→ 150 applications per week 
→ 30+ cold messages daily
→ Ready to unpaid work for experience 
→ Treating job search like my job

Than within 3 weeks came the email that changed everything.
"We're pleased to offer you a job as a Business Analyst..."

Finally, my journey made sense!

4 years later: 
→ Debt: Cleared 
→ Job: Data Analytics Manager
→ Community: 100,000+ on LinkedIn 
→ Featured on: Times Square billboard
→ Featured in: Business Insider, CNBC, Fox News
→ Fulfilled my dream: Gave a TEDx speech
→ Impact: Helping thousands of students succeed 

The truth about putting everything on the line:
Yes, it's terrifying. Yes, you'll question it daily. Yes, you might fail.

But playing it safe guarantees you'll never discover what you're truly capable of.

My biggest risk became my biggest reward.

For everyone betting everything on their dreams - your struggle is temporary, your growth will be permanent. 

You've got this, Keep going!

--- Post 343 ---
Author: sohansethi
Date: Apr 17, 2026
Engagement: 81 reactions, 37 comments, 2 reposts (Score: 779)
Media: image
URL: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7444032111310729216

A candidate I know got rejected by the same company twice.

Then got hired there 6 months later.

Here is what changed and it was not his skills.

The first time, his resume was solid. SQL, Python, Power BI. But every bullet read like a job description.

"Responsible for creating dashboards."
"Assisted in data analysis for business teams."

The hiring manager could not tell what he actually did. What changed because he was there.

She moved on in 30 seconds.

The second time he updated his resume. Better bullets. Numbers this time. But he bombed the interview.

She asked: "Tell me about a time your analysis changed a business decision."

He walked her through the methodology. Explained the model he built.

He never told her what the business did with it.

She passed again.

Six months later he emailed her directly. Not through the portal. An actual email.
It said three things:

-- He mentioned a specific project her team had published
-- He attached a case study where his analysis led to a $200K cost reduction
-- He asked for 15 minutes. Not a job. Just a conversation.

She gave him the meeting.

Same question. Same candidate.
This time he answered before she finished asking.

"Last quarter I found a $200K inefficiency in our vendor contracts. My manager presented it to the CFO and we renegotiated the deal. I can walk you through exactly how I found it."

She hired him two weeks later.

-- First rejection - positioning problem. Had the skills. Could not show the impact.
-- Second rejection - communication problem. Could do the work. Could not explain why it mattered.
-- Third attempt - he fixed both. Then reached out directly with proof instead of promises.

Skills get you in the room.
Communication gets you the offer.
Initiative makes a hiring manager remember you.

Which one are you still working on?
