--- Post 353 ---
Author: sohansethi
Date: Apr 17, 2026
Engagement: 79 reactions, 33 comments, 4 reposts (Score: 733)
Media: image
URL: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7445836467349651456

If you want to clear any Data Analyst interview, these 15 questions are most likely to come up.

I have conducted over 100 interviews. These are the ones I ask every single time.

Save this. Study every one of them.

𝗧𝗲𝗰𝗵𝗻𝗶𝗰𝗮𝗹 𝗦𝗤𝗟 𝗤𝘂𝗲𝘀𝘁𝗶𝗼𝗻𝘀

What is the difference between WHERE and HAVING?
-- WHERE filters before grouping. HAVING filters after. Mixing these up is an immediate red flag.

Write a query to find the second highest salary in a table
-- Strong candidates use a window function. The approach tells me exactly where you are in your SQL journey.

What is the difference between INNER JOIN and LEFT JOIN?
-- INNER returns only matching rows. LEFT returns all rows from the left table even without a match.

What are window functions and when would you use them?
-- RANK, ROW_NUMBER, LAG, LEAD. If you cannot explain these with a real use case you are not ready for a mid-level role.

What is the difference between DELETE, TRUNCATE, and DROP?
-- DELETE removes specific rows. TRUNCATE removes all rows. DROP removes the table entirely.

𝗣𝘆𝘁𝗵𝗼𝗻 & 𝗗𝗮𝘁𝗮 𝗤𝘂𝗲𝘀𝘁𝗶𝗼𝗻𝘀

How do you handle missing values in a dataset?
-- fillna() to fill, dropna() to remove. The real answer depends on business context. Always explain your reasoning.

What is the difference between loc and iloc?
-- loc uses labels. iloc uses integer positions. Simple but candidates confuse them under pressure.

How do you merge two datasets with different column names?
-- pd.merge() with left_on and right_on. Frequently tested and trips up candidates who only practiced on clean data.

𝗕𝘂𝘀𝗶𝗻𝗲𝘀𝘀 & 𝗔𝗻𝗮𝗹𝘆𝘁𝗶𝗰𝗮𝗹 𝗧𝗵𝗶𝗻𝗸𝗶𝗻𝗴

Walk me through how you would approach a 20% sales drop last month
-- Segment by region, product, channel, time. Isolate the variable. Do not jump to conclusions before the data tells you something.

How do you decide which metrics matter for a business problem?
-- Start with the business goal. Work backwards to the metric.

Tell me about a time your analysis directly changed a business decision
-- The most important question in the interview. Have a specific example ready.

How do you explain a complex finding to a non-technical stakeholder?
-- Lead with the business impact. Save the methodology for questions.

What is the difference between correlation and causation?
-- Correlation means two things move together. Causation means one causes the other. Every analyst must understand this.

The technical questions get you to the final round.
The business questions get you the offer.

Which of these would you struggle with most?

♻️ Repost to help someone preparing for a data analyst interview
💭 Tag someone interviewing for data roles right now
📩 Get my full interview prep guide: https://lnkd.in/gjUqmQ5H

Author: sohansethi
Date: May 17, 2025

URL: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7221161820286328832

The real CHALLENGES in the Life of an International Student,
(And how you can overcome them):

Just 2 years ago, I was:
-- Staying in India
-- Unhappy with my basic job
-- Living a mediocre life
-- Striving for financial success by quitting my job and dreaming of life abroad

A lot has changed in the last 2 years.

I fulfilled my dream of living abroad and building an meaningful life; however, the journey was totally opposite to what I had imagined.

It was full of obstacles and a roller-coaster of emotions.

Here's how my life changed in the last 2 years and how I navigated the challenges that came my way:

1. Living in a new country made me fully self-reliant and independent.
- Be prepared to do everything from cooking, groceries, bills, setting up a new apartment, etc. (Even though it sounds uncomfortable, the system here is very organized and helps facilitate the change.)

2. Living on my own helped me gain invaluable skills and become confident.
- Don't be afraid to start a conversation or ask questions. Soak in the experience and learn as much as you can.

3. Pursuing a life abroad helped me step on a journey of financial freedom.
- You'll spend and earn an immense amount of money in a foreign country. Make sure to do budgeting from day one, no matter the background you come from. It will surely help you in the end.

4. Living alone made me realize I can be happy no matter where I am.
- Life abroad can be a lonely experience at times. Make sure to keep in touch with your family and friends back home. They will be your biggest support system.

5. Fulfilling my dreams left me with tons of memories and an immense feeling of gratitude.
- In the end, make sure to make the most of your life. The challenges will always exist, nevertheless, travel, soak in the experience, and make memories that will stay with you for a lifetime.

Overall, my life completely changed the moment I set foot on that airplane and my journey of life abroad.

Coming to a new country is an uncomfortable space but never try finding comfort in any of these experiences. Although tough at times, it was one of the turning points of my life and the most fulfilling one indeed.

To anyone about to get started on this journey or already in the middle of one, I know it can be overwhelming at times.
But trust me, every one around you would be feeling the same inside. 

Take this post as a reminder that you've got this, you're capable enough and the life you dream of is exactly a step away.

I Believe in you, take care, and keep growing!

P.S: This was my 2nd out of 7 posts in my latest series of Life as a New International Student. If you are interested to learn more on the best tips and tricks to tackle life in a foreign country, like this post and stay tuned for the next few weeks!

--- Post 362 ---
Author: sohansethi
Date: Oct 19, 2025
Engagement: 115 reactions, 12 comments, 2 reposts (Score: 699)
Media: image
URL: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7383862504008945664

The biggest mistake I made while coming to the USA:

I did zero research on the visa process, thinking getting a Green Card would be a straightforward journey.

However, here's what the reality looked like:

→ You start with F-1 student visa.
→ Apply for CPT to gain work experience during studies. 
→ After graduation, you get OPT - 1 year work authorization. 
→ STEM majors get 24 months extension (3 years total). 
→ You pray to find an employer to sponsor your H1-B. 
→ You enter the H1-B lottery - 26% selection rate 
→ If rejected, try again next year (if OPT hasn't expired). 
→ If selected, you wait 15-20 years for Green Card.

Here is the part nobody talks about:

→ H1-B is lottery-based. Merit doesn't matter. 
→ You are tied to one employer. 
→ If you face a Layoff, you get 60 days to find a job or leave.
→ You cannot start your own company. 
→ 15-20 year Green Card wait for India/China. 

However, here's what I recently discovered:
There's a better alternative → the EB-1A Visa

EB-1A gives you a direct Green Card in 1-2 years. 
No lottery. No waiting. And it's achievable to get too!

Why the EB-1 A is can be a better alternative for you:
1. Merit-based, not luck-based
2. Green Card in 1-2 years vs 15-20 years
3. Self-sponsored - no employer control
4. Full work freedom - switch jobs, start businesses
5. Your spouse can work immediately too

The Best Part? You need just 3 out of 10 below criteria:

— Awards/Recognition: 
Industry awards, hackathon wins, or academic honors 

— Professional Memberships: 
Associations requiring outstanding achievement 

— Published Articles: 
Research papers or technical articles in reputed journals 

— Judging Others' Work: 
Peer reviews, competition judging, or code reviews 

— Patents/Contributions: 
Patents, high-impact open-source projects, or innovations 

— Media Coverage: 
Featured in press, interviews, or news articles about you 

— High Salary: 
Earnings in top 10% for your field and location 

— Leadership Roles: 
Tech lead, director positions at reputable companies 

— Scholarly Citations: 
Your research cited by other experts in your field 

— Exhibitions: 
Showcasing your work at conferences or professional events

Many high-achievers already qualify but don't realize it!
The EB-1A visa can be the right choice for you, but you need to speak to a lawyer to get specific legal advice.

BONUS: Get experienced help for your O-1 visa, with trusted lawyers by Manifest Law
They even offer a get your Visa Approved or Money Back guarantee (for certain plans; conditions apply).

Begin your O-1 journey today with a free consultation with Manifest Law: https://lnkd.in/gQpAmBb7

I have sat on both sides of the hiring table.

As a Data Analytics Manager, I have reviewed hundreds of resumes, conducted dozens of interviews, and rejected candidates who were fully qualified - simply because they did not know how to present themselves.

The job search is not fair. 
But it is learnable.

The candidates winning right now are using AI as a full job search system - not just a writing assistant.

Here are 10 Claude prompts that actually move the needle 👇
Prompt 1 - Find Jobs That Match Your Profile 
-- Tell Claude your degree, experience, skills, and target industry 
-- Get 10 matching roles, required skills, salary ranges, and exact titles recruiters use

Prompt 2 - Find Companies That Sponsor Visas 
-- Stop applying to companies that never intended to sponsor 
-- Get 20 visa-friendly companies, why they hire international talent, and tips to increase your chances

Prompt 3 - Optimize Your Resume for ATS 
-- Most resumes are rejected before a human reads them 
-- Paste your resume + job description. Claude rewrites bullets with action verbs, metrics, and the right keywords

Prompt 4 - Create Powerful Resume Bullet Points 
-- Weak bullets cost you interviews 
-- Format: action verb + task + measurable result. Claude rewrites them with impact and business value

Prompt 5 - Write a Personalized Cover Letter 
-- Generic cover letters get ignored 
-- Claude writes a targeted letter with your achievements and alignment to the company mission

Prompt 6 - Prepare for Interviews 
-- Claude simulates a real interview for your exact role 
-- 15 technical questions, 10 behavioral, 5 case-study - with sample strong answers

Prompt 7 - Improve Your LinkedIn Profile 
-- Recruiters are already checking your profile before they call you 
-- Claude rewrites your headline, About section, and experience with keywords recruiters search for

Prompt 8 - Write Networking Messages 
-- "I'd love to connect" is not a strategy 
-- Claude writes personalized outreach for recruiters, hiring managers, and alumni that actually gets responses

Prompt 9 - Prepare for Salary Negotiation 
-- Most candidates accept the first offer. That is a mistake 
-- Get the market range, negotiation strategy, email template, and a list of benefits to negotiate beyond base salary

Prompt 10 - Build a Job Search Tracker 
-- A structured system beats random applications every time 
-- Claude builds a full tracker in Excel or Notion - company, role, date, stage, contact, follow-up date

Save this. Use every prompt. Make your search intentional.
Which prompt are you using first?

Author: sohansethi

Engagement: 83 reactions, 26 comments (Score: 675)

The hardest thing I experienced as an International Student:

I sacrificed everything and still did not see any results!

During my job search, there was a time when I applied to 200+ applications, sent out cold messages, attended job fairs, and gave 2-3 interviews every single week, on repeat.

The result: Failing again and again. 
And I am not alone.

Almost two out of three people I spoke with faced the same struggle.

As an International Student searching for a job, You: 
→ Follow every advice 
→ Fix your resume 20+ times 
→ Network like crazy as they tell you 
→ Apply to thousands of jobs 
→ Do everything that can possibly be done.

And yet do not receive any callbacks.

That's the reality. And it's okay to admit that this is hard.

Over 9 months of my job search, I had submitted 3000+ applications, given 40+ interviews, and got rejected multiple times in the final round.

I had already graduated, my savings were getting exhausted, my loan payments were on my head, and I was deeply broken with this brutal job search.

At that time, I asked myself: How much longer can I keep doing this? 
The honest answer: I didn't know.

However, the next week, I applied to 200+ jobs again.

Luckily, I got an interview and prepared my best for this role. 
I went through 5 rounds of interviews and Finally Got the job!

Looking back, I know just 1 thing: If I had given up at my lowest, I wouldn’t have been able to do what I do right now.

-- Today, 2 years later, I am working as a Manager in the same company. 
-- Have built a community of 120,000+ on LinkedIn 
-- Impacted millions and helped them get better jobs 
-- Featured in TEDx, CNBC News, Business Insider, and many more.

Honestly, those 3500+ applications don’t even matter now. 
The only thing that matters is the journey and impact I get to create today.

To everyone in the middle of their job search right now:
-- I know you are tired 
-- I know you are improving yourself 
-- I know you are doing everything you can

This is the hardest part.
Not the failure. But continuing after repeated failure.

But here's what I know after 3,000 rejections:
The tide WILL turn. Not because you're special, But because tides always turn.
That's nature. That's life. That's how this works. Your job isn't to know when. 

Your job is to still be standing when it does.

One more day. One more application. One more try.
That's all you need.

Your breakthrough is coming. Just don't quit before it arrives. 
And before you know it, your offer letter will be in your hands soon!

I hope my story was helpful.
I believe in you, keep going, all the best!

P.S: If my story resonated with you, follow me on Instagram for more such impactful stories and job search advice daily: https://lnkd.in/gsxeKsKn

→ Filter by Posts 
→ Use "All Filters" for specific companies

→ Keep it short & specific
→ Mention the exact role you’re applying for
→ Show company knowledge (Not just “I love Tesla” – be specific!)
→ Request a quick chat, NOT just a referral

FREE Harvard University Resume and Cover Letter Samples

This template has helped 1000’s of job seekers increase callbacks:

The Winning Resume Structure:

1. Education → Show the knowledge foundation you bring 
2. Skills → Highlight what you can contribute from day one
3. Work Experience → Prove how your background solves their problems 
4. Projects → Demonstrate expertise across diverse skill sets 
5. Leadership & Awards → Showcase your complete professional personality

Resume Optimization Tips: 
✅ Under 10 years experience? Keep it to 1 page 
✅ Pack every bullet with metrics and data 
✅ Use this formula: "Accomplished [X] as measured by [Y], by doing [Z]" 
✅ Tailor each resume for ATS optimization

The Winning Cover Letter Structure:

1. Opening Hook → Grab attention + mention specific role 
2. The Connection → Show genuine company research and interest
3. Value Proposition → 2-3 key achievements with metrics that match their needs 
4. The Bridge → Connect your experience to their success 
5. Strong Close → Express enthusiasm + request next steps

Cover Letter Optimization Tips:
✅ Keep it 3-4 paragraphs max (6-second scan rule!)
✅ Mirror job posting keywords for ATS
✅ Use this power formula: "At [Company], I [achievement + numbers] = [quantifiable impact]"
✅ Research the hiring manager's name on LinkedIn (40% higher response rate!)

Your application is your first impression. Make it count!
Implement this system for 2-3 weeks and watch your callback rate skyrocket.

BONUS: If you're struggling with crafting a ATS Friendly resume, Try
Wisedoc AI Recruiting's AI tools to optimize your profile and increase callbacks in your job search.
Try it here: https://wisedoc.net/

Remember: Quality applications beat quantity every time.

How to get ahead of 99% competition in the current job market:

- Go quiet for 3 months

- SET a goal to secure an X role with a Y salary 

- Stop self-comparison and FOCUS on your journey

- Seek excellence in 3-5 key skills for your target role

- Do tons of Information Interviews to learn industry best practices

- Record each interview, learn from your flaws, and do better the next time.

- Lastly, Apply to roles as much as POSSIBLE (Without compromising Quality)

Most Important: Every 2 weeks; Self-evaluate, acknowledge that you are doing everything that you possibly can, be proud of your efforts, and practice GRATITUDE.

"I just found out I'll be 50 years old when I finally get my Green Card.
I'm 33 right now, That's 17 years of waiting."

This is what my friend just told me:
-- 17 years tied to one employer
-- 17 years of entrepreneurial dreams on hold
-- 17 years of spouse unable to work freely.

This is the reality for thousands of H1-B holders from India and China.
And nobody talks about it.

Here's what the "Normal Journey" actually looks like:
→ Graduate with a top degree and land a great job. 
→ Win the H1-B lottery (26% chance). 
→ Apply for Green Card and join the queue. 
→ Wait 15-17 years due to country quotas. 
→ Stay with same employer or restart. 
→ Watch your life pass by.

But here's what I recently discovered:
You don't have to wait 17 years. There's another path.

The EB-1 visa gives you a Green Card in 1-2 years.
No lottery. No employer dependency. No country quotas.

Here are the advantages of the EB-1: 
-- No lottery system (merit-based)
-- No country quotas or backlogs
-- Self-sponsored option available
-- 1-2 year processing time
-- Full career freedom from day one 
-- Switch jobs or start a business freely

If you're a senior engineer, researcher, or high-achiever in your field, you likely already meet the minimum criteria to qualify for an EB-1 Visa.

Most high-performers qualify but don't know this path exists.

An experienced immigration lawyer can evaluate your profile and build a strong EB-1 case for you.

3 Leadership Lessons I Learned in My First 60 Days as a Manager:

I recently stepped into a manager role, and it completely changed how I think about leadership!

Here are 3 lessons that have shaped my mindset:

1. Ownership is Key:
— Leaders always own their mistakes and the solutions.
— If it fails, it’s on me. If it wins, it’s us.
— As a manager, the spotlight isn’t always on success, it’s on how you handle setbacks.

2. Build Trust:
— Trust is the real currency of leadership.
— People don’t follow managers, they follow leaders they trust.
— Trust is built in small moments: listening actively, being transparent, showing up.

3. Lead by Example:
— Your team mirrors your mindset.
— If you raise the bar, others will too.
— Bring energy. Be present. Set the bar with your effort, your attitude, your discipline and your team will follow.

I've realized being a Leader isn't about being the smartest person in the room anymore.
It's about bringing everyone together and getting the best out of each individual.

Also, I believe not just the corporate world,
The above lessons can be applied to our daily lives too!

Do you agree?
Let me know your favorite leadership lesson in the comments below 👇

Coming to the USA for my master’s was the WORST decision of my life,
It has been the most painful choice I’ve made:

— I am always worried about finding a job
— I am always worried about getting an H1B visa
— I am always worried about my huge student loan
— I am always worried about making ends meet

If this wasn’t enough, I am constantly anxious because:

— I haven’t seen my family for years
— I miss the support of my childhood friends
— I love the comfort of life back home

These were the thoughts running through my head almost 2 years ago.

However, instead of succumbing to these thoughts at the peak of my job-search process, a sudden shift happened in my life:

I realized I was responsible for my own future, and no one was coming to save me!
This is when I became proactive.

These are the strategies I followed to manage stress and keep moving forward:

1. Set realistic goals:
Instead of trying to apply to 50 jobs in one day, I aimed for 15 high-quality applications.

2. Created a routine:
I treated my job search like a job. I allocated specific hours each day for networking, applying, and upskilling.

3. Celebrated small wins:
Celebrate small milestones because they’re signs of progress too.

4. Practice self-care:
I set aside time for exercise, hobbies, or simply relaxing with a book or my favorite show.

5. Seeked support:
Reach out to mentors, friends, or connections. Your network is always there to support you.

Remember, stress is natural, but it doesn’t have to derail your journey.
And just like everything else, it is temporary too!

It was only after facing these tough situations that I ultimately:

— Got my dream job in a great U.S company
— Shared my journey on a TEDx stage
— Built a 125,000+ community on LinkedIn (this I am most grateful for)

If I had the chance, I would go through that pain all over again.

I am sure many of you have felt like how I was feeling just 2 years ago.
If you’re stuck now, remember that every setback is a setup for an incredible future.

Life’s toughest seasons often lead to the most beautiful breakthroughs.

P.S: If you're an international student reading this, it is very easy to lose track and feel overwhelmed due to visa uncertainty.

However, you don't have to carry this weight alone.

It is a free community of students pursuing O-1/EB-1 visas. You get access to exclusive events and courses, with the opportunity to grow your network.

Here are 20 of the most common interview questions to prepare for your next interview: 

1. Tell me about yourself
2. Why do you want this job?
3. What’s your biggest weakness?
4. Tell me about a failure
5. Describe a time you disagreed with your manager
6. How do you handle stress or pressure?
7. Why are you leaving your current job?
8. What motivates you?
9. Tell me about a conflict with a coworker
10. Where do you see yourself in 5 years?
11. Describe your ideal work environment
12. How do you prioritize your tasks?
13. What do you know about our company?
14. How do you define success?
15. Tell me about a project you’re proud of
16. What would your colleagues say about you?
17. How do you handle tight deadlines?
18. What are your salary expectations?
19. Why should we hire you?
20. Do you have any questions for us?

Pro Tip: Interview success = Preparation × Self-Awareness × Authenticity

Author: sohansethi

Media: image
URL: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7277338264628305921

7 Things You Should NOT Say in a Job Interview:
(And What To Say Instead, To Get An Offer)

Interviews are like first dates:
Make a good impression, and you're one step closer to landing the job (or a second date!). 

But certain phrases can make or break a good interview, ultimately wasting all your preparation and efforts. 

Here are 7 things to avoid saying in a job interview:

1. "I don't know." - It’s okay not to have all the answers, but try rephrasing in a more positive sense. 
Instead Say, - “I’ll look into that” or “I’m eager to learn.”

2. "I just need a job." - Employers want candidates who are passionate about the role. 
Instead - Highlight why you’re specifically interested in their company.

3. "What does your company do?" - Basic research is crucial. 
Instead - Convey to the interviewer that you’re serious by knowing their mission and recent projects.

4. "My last boss was terrible." - Even if it’s true, stay positive. Speaking ill of a former employer may raise red flags.
Instead - Give genuine reasons for the negative and positive aspects of the work culture.

5. "I don’t have any questions." - Not asking questions can come across as disinterest. 
Instead - Prepare thoughtful questions about the company or role, and ask them at the end of the interview.

6. I’ll do anything”- Portraying yourself as too needy makes you lose your leverage as a candidate. 
Instead say: I am passionate about [XYZ]. How can I leverage my skills to [XYZ] area in the company?

7. "I have no weaknesses." - Avoid perfectionism! It often conveys exactly the opposite of a growth mindset.
Instead - Share a genuine area for growth and how you’re addressing it recently.

Lastly, remember that every interaction is an opportunity to showcase your best self. 

Be prepared, show interest, and let your authentic self shine. 
With the right preparation, I’m sure you’ll land the right opportunity soon.

"Managed team projects" → too vague 
"Led cross-functional initiatives" → sounds like everyone else 
"Responsible for customer support" → boring, generic & forgettable

Every bullet point looked the same.
Every rejection felt like proof that my work didn't matter.

That’s when I changed my approach and got interview from top companies.

I’ve realized everyone building tools for job seekers was solving the wrong problem.
They were building ATS scanners, LinkedIn optimizers. Template libraries with 500 generic examples that sound like they were written by a robot.

But nobody was solving the one thing that actually gets you interviews: showing your work has impact.
So I built one!

Here's what my tool does:
→ Paste your boring bullet point 
→ It asks what you actually accomplished (with numbers) 
→ Rewrites it in the "Accomplished [X] as measured by [Y] by doing [Z]" format → Adds action verbs that recruiters actually scan for 
→ Gives you 3 variations (conservative, moderate, bold) 
→ Shows you exactly which keywords from the job description are missing

Here's an example from my own resume:
Before - "Managed daily operations for automation" 
After - "Reduced processing time by 40% by implementing automated workflow, saving 15 hours/week across 5-person team."

Same work. Completely different impact!

I'm 29 years old, and I'm a failure.

Not because I didn't achieve my goals, But because I achieved the wrong ones:

Here’s what success looks like from the outside:
-- Having a six-figure salary
-- 120,000+ LinkedIn followers 
-- Fancy car and a nice apartment 
-- Visiting new countries and experiences
-- Featured on TEDx, CNBC and Times Square

According to society: I’ve made it!

However, have you ever felt empty even after having everything?
I sure have, more now than ever before.

Expectations and comparison are the biggest thieves of happiness.

Over the past few months, this is what I have realized:
I was chasing the wrong success metrics!

Here’s what success really looks like:
-- Spending real quality time with family
-- (not just sending money back home)

-- Having a healthy mind and body
-- (not just a busy calendar throughout the week)

-- Helping people genuinely and changing lives
-- (not just posting for LinkedIn engagement)

-- Finding inner peace through spirituality
-- (not just a fancy lifestyle on Instagram)

To everyone chasing what I chased:
The salary is great - Get it. 
The title feels good - Earn it. 
The recognition is nice - Enjoy it.

But don't sacrifice your peace for it. 
Don't lose your family for it. 
Don't forget yourself in the process.

P.S: If you're an international student reading this, it is very easy to lose track and feel overwhelmed due to visa uncertainty.

However, you don't have to carry this weight alone.

Find your community. Share your struggles. Build together.

I learned this networking strategy from a PM at Amazon.

Then this strategy helped my get multiple FAANG interviews:

Today, Informational interviews are key for getting callbacks. 
Here's my step-by-step guide to get tons of Informational interviews:

𝗦𝘁𝗲𝗽 𝟭: 𝗙𝗶𝗻𝗱 𝗣𝗲𝗼𝗽𝗹𝗲 𝗪𝗵𝗼 𝗪𝗔𝗡𝗧 𝘁𝗼 𝗛𝗲𝗹𝗽
Not everyone is approachable. But here's who actually responds:

-- Find professionals who actively post on LinkedIn 
-- Approach people 3-7 years into their career (not VPs/Directors) 
-- Use LinkedIn's "People also viewed" feature for solid profiles
-- Reach out to alumni from your school (Best response rate)

𝗦𝘁𝗲𝗽 𝟮: 𝗧𝗵𝗲 "𝗪𝗮𝗿𝗺-𝗨𝗽" 𝗦𝘁𝗿𝗮𝘁𝗲𝗴𝘆
Don't cold message directly, provide value first!

Week 1: Engage with 3-4 of their posts (genuine comments) 
Week 2: Send a thoughtful connection request referencing their content/work
Week 3: Message them requesting for a 15 minute informational chat

This feels like continuing a conversation, not starting one.

𝗦𝘁𝗲𝗽 𝟯: 𝗧𝗵𝗲 𝗠𝗲𝘀𝘀𝗮𝗴𝗲 𝗧𝗵𝗮𝘁 𝗚𝗲𝘁𝘀 𝗥𝗲𝘀𝗽𝗼𝗻𝘀𝗲𝘀
Feel free to steal my exact template:

"Hi Sohan,
Your recent post about [specific topic] really resonated - especially [specific detail they mentioned].

I'm currently [brief context: transitioning to/learning/building skills in] data analytics and have a specific question about [their experience/tool/approach].
Would you be open to a 15-minute call next week? 
Happy to work around your schedule.

Looking forward to learning from you!

Thanks, 
[Your Name]"

𝗪𝗵𝘆 𝗧𝗵𝗶𝘀 𝗪𝗼𝗿𝗸𝘀:
✅ References their actual content (shows you're paying attention) 
✅ ONE specific question (not "tell me everything") 
✅ Time-bound commitment (15 minutes, not open-ended)

𝗗𝘂𝗿𝗶𝗻𝗴 𝘁𝗵𝗲 𝗖𝗮𝗹𝗹:
-- Prepare 5-6 specific questions 
-- Take notes (shows you value their time) 
-- Ask: "Whats a major problem you're solving at work today?" 
-- End with: "Anyone else you'd recommend I talk to?"

𝗔𝗳𝘁𝗲𝗿 𝘁𝗵𝗲 𝗖𝗮𝗹𝗹:
Within 24 hours, send this:
"Thanks for taking the time yesterday, Sohan. 
Your insight about [specific point] was really valuable - I'm already [action you're taking].
If there's ever anything I can help with, let me know!"

𝗧𝗵𝗲 𝗥𝗲𝘀𝘂𝗹𝘁:
In my experience, 9 out of 10 times, they themselves will give you a referral/interview opportunity upfront!

𝗣𝗿𝗼 𝗧𝗶𝗽: Stop networking to get something. Start networking to learn something new and the results will follow.

Author: sohansethi
Date: Mar 18, 2026
Engagement: 88 reactions, 4 comments, 1 reposts (Score: 482)
Media: image
URL: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7431713558922444800

This is how to crack a $250,000 salary job:

(Resume secrets shared by a FAANG Recruiter below):

You only have ~6 seconds to get a job!

That’s the average time a recruiter spends on your resume before making a decision.

Here’s how to maximize those 6 seconds with strategies most candidates ignore:

1. Beat the ATS before you beat the recruiter:

70–80% of resumes get filtered out by ATS.

-- Use tools like Wisedoc AI Recruiting to compare your resume against the JD and identify missing keywords.
-- Don’t “keyword dump.” Place them naturally in your Work Experience & Skills sections.

2. Tailor in minutes, not hours:

Recruiters can catch generic resumes instantly.

-- Instead of rewriting from scratch, build a master resume.
-- For each application, adjust just 3 things:
A. Headline/title
B. Top 3 bullet points
C. Skills section

That alone increases relevance by 50%+.

3. Numbers > narratives. Always:

Recruiters look for impact. If your bullets don’t quantify results, they blend into white noise.

-- Bad: “Responsible for customer support.”
-- Good: “Handled 50+ support tickets/day, reducing average resolution time by 30%.”
 
If you don’t have hard numbers, use proxies: scale (“served 200+ clients”), frequency (“led weekly workshops”), or efficiency (“cut process time by half”).

4. Design for scanning, not reading:

"Recruiters skim in an “F-pattern.”

-- Place your strongest metrics in the first 2 lines of each section.
-- Use 10–12pt font, no fancy graphics, and clear section headers.
-- Stick to formats that ATS can parse (PDF/Word, no text boxes).

5. Balance skills with story:

Yes, technical skills matter, but soft skills tip the scale.
In fact, LinkedIn data shows 89% of failed hires are due to poor culture fit.

Weave them into your achievements:
“Led a 5-member cross-functional team to deliver project 3 weeks early.” → shows leadership + adaptability without buzzwords.

BONUS: You can ace your next interview by preparing using Wisedoc AI Recruiting.
Start Preparing Here: https://lnkd.in/gt-Qq5Y7

Remember: A resume isn’t your life story.

It’s a marketing document designed to pass filters, prove value, and earn you the interview.

How to level up from $80K to $150K as a Data Analyst:

It is not the SQL.

I have managed data teams and interviewed 50+ analysts.

Here is what actually separates them:
(Save this - most people figure this out too late)

1. They speak business, not just data
-- The $80K analyst answers the question asked
-- The $150K analyst answers the question behind the question

2. They say no to bad requests
-- The $80K analyst builds every dashboard requested
-- The $150K analyst asks what decision this will drive first

3. They communicate findings like a story
-- The $80K analyst sends a spreadsheet and waits
-- The $150K analyst walks the room through the so-what in 3 slides

4. They understand the P&L
-- The $80K analyst knows their data inside out
-- The $150K analyst knows how their data connects to revenue and cost

5. They have a point of view
-- The $80K analyst presents findings and asks what to do next
-- The $150K analyst presents findings and tells you what they recommend

6. They make their manager look good
-- The $80K analyst completes tasks on time
-- The $150K analyst brings problems with solutions already attached

7. They treat visibility as a skill
-- The $80K analyst does great work and assumes people will notice
-- The $150K analyst makes sure the right people always know about it

8. They think in decisions, not dashboards
-- The $80K analyst measures everything and reports it all
-- The $150K analyst asks what decision this data is meant to change

9. They build relationships outside their team
-- The $80K analyst is known by their manager and immediate team
-- The $150K analyst is known by product leads, finance, and executives

10. They stay 6 months ahead of their role
-- The $80K analyst waits for their company to train them
-- The $150K analyst is always learning before they need to


The hard truth:
The gap is almost never the SQL.

It is how you think, how you communicate, and how much of the business you understand.
Start working on those three things today.

Save this post to Become a Data Analyst in 2025:

(Check out FREE Resources below)

Learning Data Analytics is not just about tools, it’s about building a full ecosystem of skills that connect data, insights, and impact.

Here’s how to structure your learning journey step-by-step :

1. Programming & Technical Skills
-- Start with Python, SQL, and Excel fundamentals.
-- Learn Pandas, NumPy, APIs, and data formats (CSV, JSON).
-- Master Git, scripting, and data documentation for workflow efficiency.

2. Mathematics & Statistics for Analytics
-- Build a foundation with probability, distributions, and regression.
-- Understand hypothesis testing, sampling, and linear algebra.
-- Learn concepts like Central Limit Theorem and ANOVA for analysis depth.

3. Data Collection & Preparation
-- Understand data sources, ETL basics, and data encoding.
-- Handle missing values, profiling, and data normalization.
-- Practice web scraping and schema documentation for real projects.

4. Exploratory Data Analysis (EDA)
-- Perform feature engineering, correlation heatmaps, and outlier detection.
-- Use Matplotlib, Seaborn, and Power BI for analysis visuals.
-- Explore anomaly detection, dimensionality reduction (PCA), and pattern analysis.

5. Data Visualization & Reporting
-- Design dashboards using Tableau and Plotly.
-- Focus on storytelling with data, KPI visualization, and infographics.
-- Apply data ethics, governance, and decision-making frameworks.

6. Business Intelligence & Decision Making
-- Learn forecasting, ROI metrics, and reporting automation.
-- Develop strategic data interpretation and real-time dashboards.
-- Gain interview prep and certifications to boost career readiness.

7. Career & Advanced Growth
-- Advance into SQL tuning, advanced Excel, and cloud analytics (AWS, Azure, GCP).
-- Work on real-world projects, case studies, and portfolio building.
-- Transition smoothly into data science or senior analytics roles.

Top Platforms to Practice SQL:
— DataLemur 🐵 (Ace the SQL & Data Interview) - https://datalemur.com/
— StrataScratch - www.stratascratch.com/
— LeetCode - https://leetcode.com/

This is the exact roadmap I would follow If I was starting my O-1 visa prep from scratch today:
(Currently The Best Alternative for the H1-B visa)

While there are many uncertainties tied to the H1-B, The O1 Visa offers a secure pathway for International students.

Here's a solid O1 visa prep roadmap for you below:

Months 1-3: Foundation
→ Self-assess against 8 O-1 criteria (find which 3 you're strongest in)
→ Consult immigration lawyer (Manifest Law offers free evaluation)
→ Create evidence tracker in Notion or Google Sheets
→ Start writing technical articles on Medium (aim for 1 per week)
→ Request LinkedIn recommendations from current/past managers

Months 4-7: Build Evidence
→ Apply to speak at conferences via Sessionize or Papercall
→ Join professional associations: IEEE, ACM, or field-specific orgs
→ Contribute to top GitHub repos or create high-impact open-source project
→ Judge hackathons on Devpost, MLH, or local tech events
→ Pitch yourself to podcasts using PodcastGuests

Months 8-10: Media & Recognition
→ Get featured in press using HARO (Help A Reporter Out)
→ Pitch guest posts to TechCrunch, Built In, or industry publications
→ Apply for industry awards: Webby Awards or similar
→ Share your work on Twitter/LinkedIn consistently (build thought leadership)
→ Document all achievements: screenshots, links, metrics, testimonials

Months 11-12: Application
→ Organize evidence by category with detailed descriptions
→ Collect 6-8 recommendation letters (managers, industry leaders, clients)
→ Get 2-3 expert opinion letters from recognized authorities
→ Work with lawyer to draft petition package
→ File with premium processing for 15 business day decision

Essential Resources:

1. Writing: Medium, Hashnode
2. Speaking: Sessionize, Papercall
3. Judging: Devpost, MLH, Kaggle
4. Media: HARO, PodcastGuests
5. Memberships: IEEE, ACM, industry associations
6. Awards: Webby, Product Hunt, AWS Community Builders

Pro Tips:
-- Start 12-18 months before you need it
-- Quality > Quantity (one great article beats ten mediocre ones)
-- Document EVERYTHING in real-time
-- Get lawyer guidance early (saves time and money)

Top 5 H1-B visa Alternatives to work in the USA:

(Save this post now for the future)

I got rejected in the H1-B Lottery Twice

After 6 months of research, I came up with 5 options that can help you keep your career going in the USA, despite visa struggles.

Here are the 5 H1-B visa alternatives I came to learn about:

1. O-1 Visa:

It's a extraordinary ability visa and a great alternative to the H1-B.
You only need to meet 3 out of 8 criteria:

→ Original Contribution (What sets you apart)
→ Critical Role (How your work distinguishes you)
→ Published articles or research papers
→ Speaking at industry conferences
→ Media coverage 
→ Judging work in your field
→ Leadership roles
→ Professional memberships 

Most professionals already qualify 80% for a O-1 visa. 

BONUS: Get expert O-1 help with Manifest Law - Visa Approved or Money Back guarantee (conditions apply).

Get a Free consultation here: https://lnkd.in/gQpAmBb7

2. H-1B Cap-Exempt:

You can skip the lottery by working at:
→ Universities and affiliated institutions
→ Nonprofit research organizations
→ Teaching hospitals
→ Government research facilities

Resources: Idealist.org, HigherEdJobs.com, USAjobs.gov

3. L-1 Transfer:

Work for a multinational company, this can be a great option.

Requirements:
→ 1 year with company abroad
→ Managerial/executive OR specialized knowledge
→ Company has US presence

Timeline: 13-15 months to US vs 5+ years waiting for H-1B

Companies actively using this: Google, Microsoft, Deloitte, PwC, smaller tech companies with global offices.

4. Free Trade Visas:

Selected visa’s if you are a national of one of the below countries:

E-3 (Australians): H-1B without lottery, 10,500 spots barely used
TN (Canadians/Mexicans): No annual limit, approved at border
H-1B1 (Chile/Singapore): 6,800 reserved spots, better odds

Pro tip: Target companies familiar with these visas.

5. J-1 Visa:

Who Qualifies:
→ Research Scholar (up to 5 years)
→ Specialist (up to 1 year)
→ Trainee (up to 18 months)

Strategy: Get US experience on J-1, then transition to H-1B or O-1. 

Companies tend to sponsor more when you're already here.

Here’s the honest truth:

H-1B is a game of chance.
These alternatives are games of strategy.

I've seen people wait 5+ years for H-1B while others used alternatives and started working in 2 months.

Your US dream doesn't have to depend on a 15% lottery.
You have options and can make the best use of them.

Please note: I found the above using my own research and this is not legal advice. For Case specific advise, please consult an immigration attorney.

I'l Master these 16 Charts and Data Visualizations:

(If I wanted to learn Data Analytics from Scratch today)

Data visualization transforms complex numbers into clear stories.
Choosing the right chart makes insights intuitive, impactful, and action-driven.

Here’s the Breakdown of Data Visualization Techniques:

Comparative Charts:
-- Used to compare data across categories or time periods.
-- Bar Chart: Tracks progress toward a goal.
-- Column Chart: Compares quantities across categories.
-- Line Chart: Highlights differences over time or groups.
-- Bullet Chart: Shows trends and progress continuously.

Distribution Charts:
Used to understand data spread and frequency.
-- Histogram: Displays frequency distribution.
-- Box Plot: Shows median, quartiles, and outliers.
-- Density Plot: Visualizes data distribution smoothly.
-- Violin Plot: Combines box and density plots.

Relationship Charts:
Used to identify connections or correlations between variables.
-- Scatter Plot: Reveals variable relationships.
-- Bubble Chart: Adds magnitude as a third dimension.
-- Heatmap: Uses colors to represent intensity.
-- Network Diagram: Displays relationships in connected data.

Composition Charts:
Used to show proportions or parts of a whole.
-- Pie Chart: Displays category share visually.
-- Stacked Bar Chart: Shows component contribution.
-- Tree Map: Visualizes hierarchical relationships.
-- Waterfall Chart: Explains cumulative sequential effects.

Remember: Data tells a story - visualization makes it unforgettable.

🚨 SCAM RED ALERT:

I'm receiving 3-4 emails + calls everyday since the last 30 days from fake "recruiters" offering interviews!

Here's how I realized its a scam:
→ Emails from generic addresses (Gmail.com, Yahoo.com - NOT @company.com) 
→ Vague job descriptions with no specific details 
→ Immediate interview requests without reviewing your profile 
→ Asks for SSN, bank details, or personal information upfront 
→ Requests payment for "background checks" or "training materials"

This isn't just about fake jobs.

Once they have your information, they can:
-- Steal your identity using SSN
-- Access your bank accounts
-- Commit tax fraud in your name
-- Sell your data on dark web

Here are a few common scams targeting NRI's/international students:

1. Fake Police/USCIS Calls: Scammers pose as immigration officers, claim visa issues, demand immediate payment or threaten deportation. 
Reality: Police never calls demanding immediate payment over phone without legitimate proof.

2. SSN Suspension Scam: Automated calls claim your SSN is "suspended due to suspicious activity" and ask you to verify details. 
Reality: Social Security Administration never suspends SSNs.

3. Apartment Rental Scams: "Landlords" offer great apartments at low prices, ask for deposits before viewing. 
Reality: Never send money without seeing the property in person.

4. Fake Tax Collection IRS impersonators threaten arrest for unpaid taxes, demand gift cards or wire transfers. 
Reality: IRS contacts you by mail first, never demands immediate payment via gift cards.

Here's how you can protect yourself:
✓ Verify company emails match official domains 
✓ Google the recruiter's name and company 
✓ Never share SSN/personal information over unexpected calls
✓ Never pay for job opportunities 
✓ Report scam to FTC: reportfraud.ftc.gov 

I wanted to share this post because I know a few friends in my network who fell prey to the above scams.
I request you to stay safe & stay vigilant.

There’s an alternative to the H-1B → the O-1 Visa

1. No annual lottery cap - apply when you are ready.

Yet I will say, It’s all a LIE!

Because, my LinkedIn doesn’t say that:
-- There was a time when I was jobless and had more than $50,000 in student loan debt.

-- There was a time when I missed major festivals, friend’s marriages, and parents’ birthdays back home.

-- There was a time when I was wiping tables and cleaning dishes as a part-time student.

-- There was a time when I felt out of place and had difficulty making new friends in a foreign country.

-- There was a time when I was stressed for months and faced endless rejections during my job search period.

Life as an international student is at times full of hard work, anxiety and unexpected struggles.

Thing's might seem sorted right now, however, just 2 years ago my life was filled with uncertainty.
I was about to graduate, didn't have a job in hand and was thinking how will I pay my bills from the next few months.

Moreover, having a feeling of not being good enough while at the same time seeing everybody else do better than me was surely the most anxious thing of all.

However, while I was feeling all these emotions, I realized this is the lowest that it can get. 

Nothing was going my way anyways and this is the worst that I will have to feel about myself.
I said to myself that I have already hit rock bottom and the only way from is here going to be up!

For that moment on, I gave up thinking about the result and only thought of taking one step at a time in the positive direction.

The only thing I was obsessed with was completing my targets each day, one day at a time. 
When done consistently, I gradually started seeing small changes in my life.

Once I got the momentum rolling, I realized my problems were not bigger than me.

Soon things started turning around and I started getting better at my interviews.

Finally, after a ton of patience and persistence, the day arrived when I received the job offer that changed everything for me.

If you have been laid off, are about to graduate or have already graduated and are looking for a job right now, I completely empathize with you.

The only request I would make to you is understand that your problems are not bigger than you and one soon you’ll be alright.

If I can do it, I truly believe that you can too. Believe in your process and I promise the right opportunity will be yours soon!

Leverage LinkedIn to land your next interview

Here's my 5 Step Strategy that works everytime:

After building my LinkedIn presence to 100,000+ followers and helping 1000’s of students, I've seen what actually works for job seekers.

Here's my 5-step system to landing your next job:

Step 1: Optimize Your Profile Like a Landing Page

How to do it:
-- Write a headline that shows your value, not just your title: "Sales Manager who increased revenue by 40%" vs "Sales Manager"
-- Add a professional headshot (profiles with photos get 21x more views)
-- Include 3-5 relevant skills in your summary with specific examples
-- Turn on "Open to Work" so recruiters can see it
Time needed: 2 hours, one-time setup

Step 2: Network and Engage with Potential Employers

How to do it:
-- Follow employees from 10-15 target companies
-- Connect with 30-50 employees each week with personalized messages
-- Find my Networking template here: https://bit.ly/3ZTs5Ob
-- Follow-up and ask for referrals to boost your job applications
Time needed: 15 minutes daily

Step 3: Create Content That Shows Your Expertise

How to do it:
-- Share one industry insight or lesson learned each week
-- Use this format: Problem + Your Solution + Result/Takeaway
-- Post on Tuesday-Thursday between 8-10 AM for maximum reach
Time needed: 30 minutes weekly

Step 4: The Strategic Outreach Method

How to do it:
-- After applying for a job, find the hiring manager on LinkedIn
-- Send a connection request: "Hi [Name], I just applied for the [Role] position and would love to connect. I'm particularly excited about [specific company initiative]."
-- Wait 2-3 days after they accept, then send a follow-up message
-- Share why you're genuinely interested in their company (mention recent news, values, or projects)
Time needed: 10 minutes per application

Step 5: Track Your Progress and Double Down on it

How to do it:
-- Check LinkedIn analytics weekly (who's viewing your profile?)
-- Note which posts get the most engagement from your target audience
-- Keep a simple spreadsheet: Company, Contact, Date Reached Out, Response
-- Send a weekly update to your network about your job search progress
Time needed: 15 minutes weekly

BONUS: Prepare for your next interview with Final Round AI's Interview Copilot
Link: https://lnkd.in/g-6-6ZPB

Remember: Consistency beats perfection.

Start with Step 1 today, add one step slowly and steadily.
I assure you, the results will soon follow.

This Cover Letter got me interviews at Google, Amazon, CVS Health, Capital One, Goldman Sachs, and many more!

Here's exactly how a winning cover letter is structured:

1. Opening Hook: 
-- Start with a compelling first line that grabs attention immediately. 
-- Mention the specific role and where you found it.

2. The Connection: 
-- Research the company and show genuine interest in their mission. 
-- Reference a recent company achievement, news, or initiative.

3. Your Value Proposition: 
-- Highlight 2-3 key achievements that directly relate to the job requirements. 
-- Use specific metrics and results to back up your claims.

4. The Bridge: 
-- Explain how your past experience translates to success in this specific role. 
-- Address any potential concerns or gaps proactively.

5. Strong Close: 
-- Express enthusiasm and request next steps. 
-- Include a professional sign-off with your contact information.

Here are the game-changing tips that make cover letters irresistible:

— Keep it to 3-4 paragraphs maximum. Hiring managers spend 6 seconds scanning it. 
-- Use the same keywords from the job posting to beat ATS systems. 
-- Follow this formula: "In my previous role at [Company], I [specific achievement with numbers], which resulted in [quantifiable impact]." 
-- Always customize for each application - generic cover letters often get ignored.

If you're currently getting rejected without interviews, implement these strategies. 
I assure that within 2-3 weeks using this method, you'll start seeing interview invitations.

I got rejected in the H1-B lottery twice...

And my 3rd attempt might be the hardest one yet:

The H-1B lottery just changed on February 27.
If you are an international student applying for an H1-B visa this March - this is the most important thing you will read today.

(Save this and share it with every international student you know)

Here is exactly what changed in the H1-B lottery and what it means for you:
1. The random lottery is gone
-- Your salary now determines your selection odds
-- Higher wage level = more entries = better chances of getting picked

2. Here is how the new wage-based system works
-- Level I (entry-level): 1 entry — lowest odds
-- Level II (mid-level): 2 entries — moderate odds
-- Level III (senior): 3 entries — strong odds
-- Level IV (expert): 4 entries — highest odds

3. Recent graduates are the most affected
-- Most new grad roles fall at Level I or Level II wages
-- 83% of historical H-1B petitions were filed at these two levels
-- That majority now has the worst odds under the new system

4. What you should do right now
-- Ask your employer what wage level your offer falls under
-- If you are at Level I, ask if there is room to move to Level II with salary adjustments.
-- Start exploring alternatives: O-1, EB-2 NIW, L-1, TN visa

The old strategy of just applying and hoping no longer works.
Plan early. Move fast. Explore every option available to you.

P.S: The above information is based on my own research, please consult an immigration lawyer for detailed legal advice.

The new H-1B wage-based rule made the lottery more harder:

You may now have a 42% lower chance of being selected in the lottery if you’re an early career professional. 

Here’s how it works:
Instead of a random lottery, your selection odds now depend on your wage level.

→ Wage Level I : 1 entry = 15.29 %
→ Wage Level II : 2 entries = 30.58 %
→ Wage Level III : 3 entries = 45.87 %
→ Wage Level IV : 4 entries = 61.16 %

What This Means for You:

If you're a recent grad or early-career professional.
→ You're likely Wage Level I or II
→ Companies will favor senior hires over new grads
→ Getting H-1B sponsorship just became much harder

Old system: ~26% random selection chance
New system: Entry-level (Wage I) positions have 42% LOWER probability.

This is where most of the early career international students will struggle.
However, There's a Better Alternative: The O-1 Visa

Unlike H-1B, the O-1:
→ Has NO lottery system
→ Has NO wage-level requirements
→ Is merit-based, not luck-based
→ Has 3-year validity and unlimited renewals
→ Gives YOU complete control over your future

The best part: It’s more achievable than you think!

You only need to fulfill 3 out of 8 criteria

Example:
-- Publications/research papers
-- Leadership roles in organizations
-- Industry awards/recognition
-- Media coverage

Many high-achievers are already 80% qualified but don't realize it.

80% of job seekers are doing their job search wrong!

People will HATE this post. But I'm calling it out regardless:
In today’s market, your qualifications matter less than your connections.

Here’ the reality no one talks about: 
-- Hiring managers hire from internal referrals first
-- Recruiters push candidates from their existing network 
-- Average employees get interviews because they went to the “Right Schools”
-- Majority of talented candidates stay unemployed for months 
-- The "Connections" game is rigged from the start

Why the system feels broken: Because It IS broken.
Here’s how you can fix this with the right approach in 6 weeks:

WEEK 1-2: RECONNECT WITH YOUR EXISTING NETWORK
Start with warm connections: 
-- Former colleagues and managers 
-- College classmates and alumni 
-- People you've worked with on projects 
-- Industry peers you've met at events

Message template: "Hi [Name], hope you're doing well! I'm exploring new opportunities in [field] and would love to catch up. Any chance you're free for a quick coffee chat this week?"

WEEK 3-4: EXPAND YOUR NETWORK STRATEGICALLY
Target these people:
-- Employees at your dream companies (use LinkedIn search)
-- Hiring managers in your target roles
-- Recruiters specializing in your industry
-- Leaders sharing content you find valuable
-- People one level above your target position

Outreach template: "Hi [Name], I've been following your work on [specific topic]. Your insights on [specific post/project] really resonated with me. I'm currently exploring opportunities in [field]—would you be open to a brief informational chat?"

WEEK 5-6: PROVIDE VALUE BEFORE ASKING
Build genuine relationships: 
-- Comment thoughtfully on their LinkedIn posts 
-- Share their content with your perspective added 
-- Introduce them to someone in your network 
-- Send relevant articles or resources 
-- Celebrate their wins publicly

Remember: Help first, ask later.

Once you have built trust, request them for referrals and job opportunities within their network/organization.
I assure you this approach will work better than any cold messaging template you find online!

Here’s the truth about networking:
It's not about WHO you know. It's about who knows WHAT YOU CAN DO.

Your goal isn't to collect 1,000 connections. 
It's to build 20-30 genuine relationships with people who understand your value.

BONUS: Once you secure an interview, You can ace your next interview by preparing using Final Round AI
Start Preparing Here: https://lnkd.in/gsCKAg7K

Start today. Message 3 people from your existing network.

The biggest mistake I made while coming to the USA:

I did zero research on the visa process, thinking getting a Green Card would be a straightforward journey.

However, here's what the reality looked like:

→ You start with F-1 student visa.
→ Apply for CPT to gain work experience during studies. 
→ After graduation, you get OPT - 1 year work authorization. 
→ STEM majors get 24 months extension (3 years total). 
→ You pray to find an employer to sponsor your H1-B. 
→ You enter the H1-B lottery - 26% selection rate 
→ If rejected, try again next year (if OPT hasn't expired). 
→ If selected, you wait 15-20 years for Green Card.

Here is the part nobody talks about:

→ H1-B is lottery-based. Merit doesn't matter. 
→ You are tied to one employer. 
→ If you face a Layoff, you get 60 days to find a job or leave.
→ You cannot start your own company. 
→ 15-20 year Green Card wait for India/China. 

However, here's what I recently discovered:
There's a better alternative → the EB-1A Visa

EB-1A gives you a direct Green Card in 1-2 years. 
No lottery. No waiting. And it's achievable to get too!

Why the EB-1 A is can be a better alternative for you:
1. Merit-based, not luck-based
2. Green Card in 1-2 years vs 15-20 years
3. Self-sponsored - no employer control
4. Full work freedom - switch jobs, start businesses
5. Your spouse can work immediately too

The Best Part? You need just 3 out of 10 below criteria:

— Awards/Recognition: 
Industry awards, hackathon wins, or academic honors 

— Professional Memberships: 
Associations requiring outstanding achievement 

— Published Articles: 
Research papers or technical articles in reputed journals 

— Judging Others' Work: 
Peer reviews, competition judging, or code reviews 

— Patents/Contributions: 
Patents, high-impact open-source projects, or innovations 

— Media Coverage: 
Featured in press, interviews, or news articles about you 

— High Salary: 
Earnings in top 10% for your field and location 

— Leadership Roles: 
Tech lead, director positions at reputable companies 

— Scholarly Citations: 
Your research cited by other experts in your field 

— Exhibitions: 
Showcasing your work at conferences or professional events

Many high-achievers already qualify but don't realize it!
The EB-1A visa can be the right choice for you, but you need to speak to a lawyer to get specific legal advice.

1. Technical Skills
Master core languages like SQL, Python, R, and SAS to query, clean, and analyze data for enterprise-level analytics.

Use Excel and Google Sheets for formulas, pivot tables, and seamless cloud-based data collaboration.

Understand hypothesis testing, regression, probability, sampling, and A/B testing to derive data-backed conclusions.

5. Machine Learning for Analysts

6. ETL & Data Processing
Streamline workflows using Airflow, Talend, Pandas, Dask, PySpark, and Alteryx for data extraction and transformation.

7. Cloud & Big Data Tools
Work with modern infrastructures like AWS, GCP, Azure, Hadoop, and Spark to handle large-scale analytics.

Communicate insights effectively using Tableau, Power BI, Looker Studio, Matplotlib, Seaborn, and Plotly.

9. Data Reporting & BI Tools
Leverage Power BI, Tableau, Looker, and Qlik Sense to build interactive, business-ready dashboards.

10. Database Management
Manage and query data efficiently with MySQL, PostgreSQL, MongoDB, Snowflake, and BigQuery.

The current H-1B Visa Lottery will soon come to an end!

A new wage-based lottery system will soon come into effect with more information to be released on 29th December, 2025.

This means many international students, especially with entry level roles will be at a disadvantage in the upcoming H-1B Lottery. 

However, what If I told you there is a Better Alternative?
The H-1B ties you to one Lottery. The O-1 gives you options!

H-1B: Lottery-based, employer-dependent, 60-day grace period risk
O-1: Merit-based, far more control over your career

It's always better to prepare for the worst case scenario in advance!
(Note: This is not legal advice, please consult a lawyer for case-based legal advice)

Here's my 12-month roadmap for the O-1 Visa:

Months 1-3: Foundation
→ Self-assess against 8 O-1 criteria (find which 3 you're strongest in)
→ Consult immigration lawyer (Manifest Law offers free evaluation)
→ Create evidence tracker in Notion or Google Sheets
→ Start writing technical articles on Medium (aim for 1 per week)
→ Request LinkedIn recommendations from current/past managers

Months 4-7: Build Evidence
→ Apply to speak at conferences via Sessionize or Papercall
→ Join professional associations: IEEE, ACM, or field-specific orgs
→ Contribute to top GitHub repos or create high-impact open-source project
→ Judge hackathons on Devpost, MLH, or local tech events
→ Pitch yourself to podcasts using PodcastGuests

Months 8-10: Media & Recognition
→ Get featured in press using HARO (Help A Reporter Out)
→ Pitch guest posts to TechCrunch, Built In, or industry publications
→ Apply for industry awards: Webby Awards or similar
→ Share your work on Twitter/LinkedIn consistently (build thought leadership)

Months 11-12: Application
→ Organize evidence by category with detailed descriptions
→ Collect 6-8 recommendation letters (professors, industry leaders, clients)
→ Get 2-3 expert opinion letters from recognized authorities
→ Work with lawyer to draft petition package
→ File with premium processing for 15 business day decision

Why Start Now:→ You have steady income for lawyer fees ($8K-$12K) 
→ You have time to build properly (not in panic mode) 
→ You have current job for recommendation letters 
→ You have peace of mind to think clearly

The truth is simple:
The H-1B was never built for career flexibility. 

The O-1, especially with an agent petitioner, gives you the freedom to move, negotiate, and grow without jeopardizing your immigration status.

BONUS TIP: An experienced immigration lawyer can evaluate your profile and build a strong O-1 case for you.

Author: sohansethi

I just found out about a Green Card pathway that doesn't require employer sponsorship:
It's called EB-2 NIW (National Interest Waiver).

Here's why it matters:

The Traditional Green Card Path looks like this:
→ Find employer willing to sponsor
→ Wait for PERM labor certification (12-18 months)
→ File I-140 petition
→ Wait 10-15 years for India/China nationals
→ Stay with same employer or restart

Now take a look at the EB-2 NIW Path:
→ You sponsor yourself (no employer needed)
→ Skip PERM process entirely
→ File I-140 directly
→ Same 10-15 year wait (BUT you control it)
→ Switch jobs freely, start businesses

The Difference is clear:

Traditional: Employer owns your Green Card process
EB-2 NIW: YOU own your Green Card process

To Qualify for EB-2 NIW, You need to prove:
1. Your work is in an area of substantial merit and national importance
2. You are well-positioned to advance that work
3. It would benefit the US to waive the job offer requirement

Real Examples of Who Qualifies:

→ Advanced degree (Master's or Bachelor's + 5 years experience)
→ Evidence your work has national importance
→ Publications, citations, or patents (helpful but not required)
→ Letters from experts in your field
→ Proof of impact (users, revenue, research adoption)

Most people don't know about EB-2 NIW because immigration lawyers prefer employer-sponsored cases (they're easier). 
Don't wait 10 years tied to one employer when you could have flexibility NOW.

BONUS: Manifest Law specializes in EB-2 NIW cases and can evaluate your eligibility.

They offer a Visa Approved or Money Back guarantee (for certain plans; conditions apply).

Author: sohansethi

URL: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7403445460692058112

9 out of 10 Job Seekers face Burnout:

Here's how I dealt with Burnout during my own Job Search:

I used to think constant exhaustion was just part of the grind!
Long hours, tight deadlines, and endless rejections felt normal, until I realized they weren’t. 

Burnout doesn’t hit all at once; 
It creeps in quietly, draining your energy, motivation, and joy without warning.

Recognizing the signs early is key to protecting your mental health and performance well in the long run.

Here are 10 Signs You’re Burning Out (and How to Fix Them)

1. Constant Fatigue
You feel tired all the time, even after sleeping.
Fix: Prioritize rest, unplug regularly, and allow your body to recover deeply.

2. Lack of Motivation
Projects feel like chores instead of challenges.
Fix: Reconnect with your purpose and set small, achievable goals to regain momentum.

3. Declining Productivity
You work longer hours but get less done.
Fix: Try focus techniques like Pomodoro or task batching to restore efficiency.

4. Emotional Numbness
You stop feeling excitement, joy, or empathy.
Fix: Reintroduce activities you love - art, music, or nature walks can help.

5. Irritability & Mood Swings
Minor inconveniences trigger frustration or anger.
Fix: Practice mindfulness, deep breathing, or take short breaks to reset your emotions.

6. Physical Symptoms
Headaches, tension, or insomnia become frequent.
Fix: Maintain a healthy routine with light exercise, balanced meals, and sleep hygiene.

7. Avoiding Responsibilities
You procrastinate or delay even simple tasks.
Fix: Break big tasks into micro-actions, focus on one step at a time.

8. Negative Self-Talk
You criticize yourself for not being “good enough.”
Fix: Replace perfectionism with kindness, talk to yourself like a friend would.

9. Disconnection from People
Isolation feels easier than socializing.
Fix: Reconnect through small, meaningful interactions, a short chat or coffee helps.

10. Growing Cynicism
You feel detached or resentful toward work or your team.
Fix: Reflect on your “why,” take rest seriously, and realign your work with your values.

I request you to recognize the signs early. 
Take intentional steps to rest, recharge, and reconnect.

Share this with someone who might need the reminder today.

I hope this helps, All the best!

Author: sohansethi

URL: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7426991528633462784

7 Things You Should NOT Say in a Job Interview:

(And What To Say To Stand Out and Get An Offer)

Interviews are like first dates:
Make a good impression, and you're one step closer to landing the job (or a second date!). 

But certain phrases can make or break a good interview, ultimately wasting all your preparation and efforts. 

Here are 7 things to avoid saying in a job interview:

1. "I don't know." - It’s okay not to have all the answers, but try rephrasing in a more positive sense. 
Instead Say, - “I’ll look into that” or “I’m eager to learn.”

2. "I just need a job." - Employers want candidates who are passionate about the role. 
Instead - Highlight why you’re specifically interested in their company.

3. "What does your company do?" - Basic research is crucial. 
Instead - Convey to the interviewer that you’re serious by knowing their mission and recent projects.

4. "My last boss was terrible." - Even if it’s true, stay positive. Speaking ill of a former employer may raise red flags.
Instead - Give genuine reasons for the negative and positive aspects of the work culture.

5. "I don’t have any questions." - Not asking questions can come across as disinterest. 
Instead - Prepare thoughtful questions about the company or role, and ask them at the end of the interview.

6. I’ll do anything”- Portraying yourself as too needy makes you lose your leverage as a candidate. 
Instead say: I am passionate about [XYZ]. How can I leverage my skills to [XYZ] area in the company?

7. "I have no weaknesses." - Avoid perfectionism! It often conveys exactly the opposite of a growth mindset.
Instead - Share a genuine area for growth and how you’re addressing it recently.

Lastly, remember that every interaction is an opportunity to showcase your best self. 

Be prepared, show interest, and let your authentic self shine. 

With the right preparation, I’m sure you’ll land the right opportunity soon.

It's a random lottery draw.
The lottery result is not your final answer.

If you're feeling uncertain about what comes next - I'm hosting a free webinar with Re-Defined, specifically for those who didn't get selected this cycle.

We'll cover: 
​-- How to evaluate which alternative path fits your profile
​-- What each option actually requires (timeline, cost, effort)
​-- Step-by-step how to get started immediately
​-- Common mistakes that can delay or derail your journey

👉 Webinar Link: https://luma.com/6wi01rvn
🗓️ Date & Time: 4th April, 1PM EST

I've watched incredibly smart, hardworking people get rejected in the lottery.

And I've watched those same people find other paths, build careers they're proud of, and stay in this country doing what they love.

I hope this helps. All the best!

I would do this to 10x my Data Analytics skills within 90 Days:

The hottest hiring season is almost here (Jan-March),
And, here’s how I would capitalize and build my analytics skills:

Step 1: Master the foundations of SQL
SQL is the backbone of data analytics. Here’s how you can master it:

1. Learn the Basics with Riti Kumari: https://lnkd.in/gBBTeF8t
2. Solve Advanced SQL Problems with Ankit Bansal: https://lnkd.in/gZj72Q_2

3. SQL Practice Platforms:
-- Mode - https://lnkd.in/gFvXVFi2
-- DataLemur 🐵 (Ace the SQL & Data Interview) - https://datalemur.com
-- Dataford - www.dataford.io
-- HackerRank - www.hackerrank.com
-- StrataScratch - www.stratascratch.com

💡 Pro Tip: Focus on mastering JOINS, aggregations, and window functions—they’re widely asked in interviews and used on the job.

Step 2: Become Fluent in Python
Python simplifies data manipulation, automation, and visualization.

1. Complete Python Live Course from freeCodeCamp: https://lnkd.in/gdF54gfw
2. Master Pandas for Data Analysis with Codebasics: https://lnkd.in/gPEbjptK

💡 Pro Tip: Use real-world datasets from Kaggle to build projects and increase your confidence.

Step 3: Bring Data to Life with Visualization
Learn to present your insights with clear, compelling visuals.

1. Tableau Tutorial for Beginners by Alex Freberg: https://lnkd.in/gAQGTj6D
2. Power BI for Dash boarding by Alex Freberg: https://lnkd.in/gyxmskc5
(Did I mention Alex twice? Ofcourse I did. Coz he's the best when it comes to data visualization!)
3. Excel Charts & Reports: https://chandoo.org/

💡 Pro Tip: Create a mini dashboard for every project on your resume to showcase your storytelling ability.

Step 4: Understand the Analytics Mindset
It’s more than tools—it’s about solving problems.

1. Learn core concepts like A/B testing, hypothesis testing, and regression analysis.
2. Statistics Core Concepts: https://lnkd.in/gpuxR8iH

💡 Pro Tip: Use simple, clear language to explain your findings.
-- Businesses love actionable insights (They basically love simplified statistical findings, that’s why analysts exist, haha!)

1. Wisedoc AI Recruiting - https://wisedoc.net/
